\displaystyle\|z_{t}\|^{2}+\nu\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{\tau}\|_{1}^{2}d\tau\leq\|z_{s}\|^{2}+\nu^{-1}\int_{s}^{t}\|f(\tau)\|^{2}d\tau.

Again applying Gronwall’s inequality to (
3.32
), one obtains

\|z_{t}\|^{2}\leq e^{-\nu(t-s)}\|z_{s}\|^{2}+\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\nu^{2}}\left(1-e^{-\nu(t-s)}\right),

which implies by using the periodicity of
z(t)
that
\|z_{t}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\nu^{2}}
for every
t
.
Now it follows from the energy inequality (
3.33
) that

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{\tau}\|_{1}^{2}d\tau\leq\nu^{-1}\|z_{s}\|^{2}+\nu^{-2}(t-s)\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\nu^{-3}\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}+\nu^{-2}(t-s)\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Therefore

I_{1}\leq C\|u_{s}\|^{2}e^{-\beta(t-s)},

where the constant
C=\exp\left(\frac{c_{0}^{2}\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\alpha\nu^{4}}\right)
, and
\beta=\nu-\frac{c_{0}^{2}\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\alpha\nu^{3}}
. Since we have assumed
G_{1}=\|f\|_{\infty}/\nu^{2}<1/c_{0}
, one can choose
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that
G_{1}<\sqrt{\alpha}/c_{0}
, hence
\beta>0
.
The estimate for
I_{1}
in turn implies that

I_{2}\leq\frac{C\varepsilon}{\beta}\left(1-e^{-\beta(t-s)}\right).

The proof is then complete by letting
\delta
sufficiently small and
T
sufficiently large so that
I_{1}+I_{2}<\frac{\sigma}{2}
for all
t\geq T
.
∎

Now we can show the weak irreducibility.

Proof of Proposition
3.9
.

By Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
, we know that for any
\sigma>0
and
R>0
, there is small enough
\delta>0
and
T_{0}(\sigma,R,\delta)>s
such that for
T>T_{0}
, we have for any
w_{0}\in B_{R}(0)
,

\mathbf{P}\left(\|w(T,s,w_{0})-z(T)\|<\sigma\right)\geq\mathbf{P}\left(\|u(T,u_{0})\|<\frac{\sigma}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|V_{T}\|<\frac{\sigma}{2}\right)\geq\mathbf{P}(\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,T})>0,

since
\left\|w_{s,t}-z_{t}\right\|\leq\left\|w_{s,t}-z_{t}-V_{t}\right\|+\left\|V_{t}\right\|=\left\|u_{t}\right\|+\left\|V_{t}\right\|
. Let
v=z(s)
. Then
z(s+nT)=v
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
since
z_{t}
is a
\mathcal{T}
-periodic solution. By replacing
T
with
s+n\mathcal{T}
for
n\in\mathbb{R}
, there is
n_{0}>0
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
, by choosing
\delta^{*}=\mathbf{P}(\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,s+n\mathcal{T}})>0
, we have

(3.34)

\displaystyle\inf_{\|w_{0}\|\leq R}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{0},B_{\sigma}(v))\geq\delta^{*}>0,

where
B_{\sigma}(v)
denotes the ball in
(H,\|\cdot\|)
with radius
\sigma
and centered at
v
.
Now for any
\varepsilon>0
, we choose
\sigma=\frac{\varepsilon}{2V(\|v\|+\varepsilon)}
, here
V(w)=e^{\eta\|w\|^{2}}
for
w\in H
is the Lyapunov function. Then
\widetilde{B}:=B_{\sigma}(v)\times B_{\sigma}(v)\subset\{(w_{1}^{\prime},w_{2}^{\prime})\in H\times H|\rho_{r}(w_{1}^{\prime},w_{2}^{\prime})<\varepsilon\}
. It now suffices to show that there is a
n_{0}>0
such that for
n>n_{0}
, there is
a>0
, and

\inf_{\|w_{1}\|,\|w_{2}\|\leq R}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}}(\widetilde{B})\geq a.

But this is equivalent to
\inf_{\|w_{1}\|,\|w_{2}\|\leq R}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1},B(v,\sigma))\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2},B(v,\sigma))\geq a
, which is a consequence of (
3.34
).
∎

As a byproduct, we have the following corollary, which is another form of weak irreducibility. By combining it with the asymptotic strong Feller property for time inhomogeneous system proposed in
[
19
]
, one can show uniqueness of periodic invariant measures. This is a generalization of the Doob and Khasminskii type argument from the seminal work
[
34
,
36
]
to the time inhomogenunous case.

Corollary 3.12
.

Assume
G_{1}<1/c_{0}
. Then for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in H
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a
v\in H
such that for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}(w_{i},B_{\varepsilon}(v))>0
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}
satisfies the asymptotic strong Feller property as in
[
19
]
, then system (
1.1
) has at most one periodic invariant measure.

Proof.

Suppose there are more than one
\mathcal{T}
-periodic invariant measure. Then there are at least two invariant measures for the associated homogenized semigroup. Let
\mu^{1}
and
\mu^{2}
be two distinct ergodic measures of
\left(\mathcal{P}_{\tau}\right)_{\tau\geq 0}
and denote the corresponding
\mathcal{T}
-periodic continuous disintegrations by
\left(\mu_{t}^{1}\right)_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
and
\left(\mu_{t}^{2}\right)_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
. Since
\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,t}\right)_{s\leq t}
is asymptotically strong Feller, it follows from Theorem 4.32 in
[
53
]
that
v\notin\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{t}^{1}\right)\cap\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{t}^{2}\right)
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
v\in H,
i.e.,
\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{t}^{1}\right)\cap

\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{t}^{2}\right)=\emptyset
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Now fix a
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and let
w_{i}\in\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{\tau}^{i}\right)
for
i=1,2
. By the weak irreducibility, there is a
v\in H
such that for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}(w_{i},B_{\varepsilon}(v))>0
. Since
\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,t}\right)_{s\leq t}
is Feller, and
B_{\varepsilon}(v)
is open, there exists
\delta>0
such that

\inf_{u\in B_{\delta}(w_{i})}\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}(u,B_{\varepsilon}(v))>0.

Since
w_{i}\in\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{\tau}^{i}\right)
, we have
\mu_{\tau}^{i}(B_{\delta}(w_{i}))>0
. Note
\mu_{\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}^{i}=\mu_{\tau}^{i}
by the
\mathcal{T}
-periodicity. Therefore

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}^{i}(B_{\varepsilon}(v))

\displaystyle=\mu_{\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}^{i}(B_{\varepsilon}(v))=\int_{H}\mathbbm{I}_{B_{\varepsilon}(v)}(u)\mu_{\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}^{i}(du)=\int_{H}\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}\mathbbm{I}_{B_{\varepsilon}(v)}(u)\mu_{\tau}^{i}(du)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{B_{\delta}(w_{i})}\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}\mathbbm{I}_{B_{\varepsilon}(v)}(u)\mu_{\tau}^{i}(du)\geq\mu_{\tau}^{i}(B_{\delta}(w_{i}))\inf\limits_{u\in B_{\delta}(w_{i})}\mathcal{P}_{\tau,\tau+n_{i}\mathcal{T}}(u,B_{\varepsilon}(v))>0

Since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary, this shows that
v\in\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{\tau}^{1}\right)\cap\operatorname{supp}\left(\mu_{\tau}^{2}\right)
, which gives the desired contradiction.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

The weak irreducibility in this subsection is inspired by that in the time homogeneous case
[
35
,
36
]
. The difference is that here we need to work with the time inhomogeneity and periodicity. The asymptotic strong Feller property proposed in
[
19
]
will be proved in Corollary
6.17
.

3.3.
Contraction on
\mathcal{P}(H)

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
2.2
, namely, the strong contraction property (
2.6
) of the transition operator
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}^{*}
when acting on
\mathcal{P}(H)
. The result can be regarded as an extension of the work of Hairer and Mattingly
[
35
]
to the time inhomogeneous setting, where they proved the existence of a unique invariant measure that is exponentially mixing under the Wasserstein metric
\rho
. The idea behind their result dates back to the early work of Dobelin
[
20
]
and Harris
[
32
]
for finite dimensional systems.
The proof here will be accomplished through a combination of the weak irreducibility from subsection
3.2
, the Lyapunov structure as in Proposition
3.1
, and the following gradient estimate.

Proposition 3.13
.

Assume
A_{\infty}=H
. Then there exists constant
\eta_{0}>0
so that for every
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
and
a>0

there exists constants
C=C(\eta,a)>0
and
p\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w)\right\|\leq C\exp(p\eta\|w\|^{2})\left(\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}|\phi|^{2}\right)(w)}+e^{-at}\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\|\nabla\phi\|^{2}\right)(w)}\right)

for every Frechet differentiable function
\phi
, every
w\in H,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\geq 0
. Here
C(\eta,a)
does not depend on initial condition
(s,w)
and
\phi
.

The proof of this proposition is much involved, which requires an infinite dimensional version of the Malliavin calculus and an infinite dimensional Hörmander type theorem. In the time homogeneous case, it has been well established in the works from
[
4
,
34
,
36
,
50
]
. Since the bounds for the solution of equation (
1.1
) behave in a uniform way with respect to the initial time, the proof of Proposition
3.13
in the time inhomogeneous case is essentially the same as that in
[
34
,
36
]
. For the reader’s convenience, we supply a proof in Appendix
6.3
.

As in
[
35
]
, to prove Theorem
2.2
, we use a metric
d
on
H
that is equivalent to
\rho
but easier to handle with the estimates from previous subsections. Recall that
r_{0}
is the constant from the Lyapunov structure in Proposition
3.1
, and
\rho_{r}
is the metric defined as in (
2.2
). For constants
r\in[r_{0},1)
,

\delta>0
and
\beta\in(0,1)
, the metric
d
is defined as

(3.35)

\displaystyle d(w_{1},w_{2})=\left(1\land\frac{\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})}{\delta}\right)+\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2}),

which is equivalent to
\rho
since
\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2})\leq d(w_{1},w_{2})\leq(\delta^{-1}+\beta)\rho(w_{1},w_{2}).

We first give a lemma that can reduce the contraction (
2.6
) to a relatively simpler case. The first part of the lemma allows us to extend the contraction of the transition operator on
H
(embedded in
\mathcal{P}(H)
) to a contraction on
\mathcal{P}(H)
. And the second part asserts that one can obtain the contraction for all times once the transition operator is a contraction at a particular time.

Lemma 3.14
.

We have

(1)

For
s\in\mathbb{R},t\geq 0
, and any given distance
d
on
H
, if
d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}})\leq\alpha d(w_{1},{w_{2}})
, for any
w_{1},{w_{2}}\in H
, then
d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{1},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{2})\leq\alpha d(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
for any
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(H)
.

(2)

If there are
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, such that for any
w_{1},{w_{2}}\in H
,
r\in[r_{0},1]
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}})\leq\alpha\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}})
. Then there are
C>0,\gamma>0
such that

\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{1},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{2})\leq Ce^{-\gamma t}\rho_{r}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}),

for any
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

Fix
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\geq 0
. By Theorem 4.4.3 from
[
43
]
on the existence of optimal couplings for probability kernels, it follows that for the transition probability kernel

\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}(\cdot,\cdot):H\times\mathcal{B}(H)\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

there is an optimal coupling kernel
Q
in the sense that

Q:\left(H\times H\right)\times\left(\mathcal{B}(H)\otimes\mathcal{B}(H)\right)\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

is a probability kernel on
\left(H\times H,\mathcal{B}(H)\otimes\mathcal{B}(H)\right)
such that
for every
(w_{1},{w_{2}})\in H\times H
,
Q((w_{1},{w_{2}})
is an optimal coupling of the transition probabilities
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}
:

(3.36)

\displaystyle d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}})=\inf_{\mu\in\mathcal{C}}\int_{H\times H}d(u,v)\mu(dudv)=\int_{H\times H}d(u,v)Q((w_{1},{w_{2}}),dudv),

where
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}})
is the set of all couplings of the transition probabilities
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}
.

Define the operator
P_{Q}
acting on
B_{b}(H\times H)
by

P_{Q}\phi(w_{1},{w_{2}})=\int_{H\times H}\phi(u,v)Q((w_{1},{w_{2}}),du,dv),

which induces an operator
P_{Q}^{*}
on
\mathcal{P}(H\times H)
by duality,
P_{Q}^{*}\mu(A\times B)=\int_{H\times H}Q((w_{1},{w_{2}}),A\times B)\mu(dw_{1}d{w_{2}})
. One can verify that if
\mu
is a coupling of
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, then
P_{Q}^{*}\mu
is a coupling of
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{2}
. Suppose
\mu_{*}
is an optimal coupling of
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
. Hence by (
3.36
),

\displaystyle d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{1},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mu\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{1},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{2})}\int_{H\times H}d(u,v)\mu(dudv)\leq\int_{H\times H}d(u,v)P_{Q}^{*}\mu_{*}(dudv)

\displaystyle=\int_{H\times H}\int_{H\times H}d(u,v)Q((w_{1},{w_{2}}),dudv)\mu_{*}(dw_{1}d{w_{2}})

\displaystyle=\int_{H\times H}d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}})\mu_{*}(dw_{1}d{w_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\int_{H\times H}d(w_{1},{w_{2}})\mu_{*}(dw_{1}d{w_{2}})=\alpha d(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).

This completes the proof for the first part of the lemma. From Lemma
6.5
, one has

\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+t}(w_{1}),\Phi_{s,s+t}({w_{2}}))\leq C\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}}),

for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\in[0,\mathcal{T}].
Observe that

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+2\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+2\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})

\displaystyle=\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s+\mathcal{T},s+2\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s+\mathcal{T},s+2\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq C\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq C^{2}\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}}).

So by iteration we have for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq C^{n}\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}}).

Now for any
0\leq t\leq N\mathcal{T}
, we can write
t=k\mathcal{T}+\beta
for unique integer
k\geq 0
such that
k\leq N
and
\beta\in[0,\mathcal{T})
. Therefore

\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})=\rho_{r}\left(\mathcal{P}_{s+\beta,s+\beta+k\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s+\beta,s+\beta+k\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}}\right)\leq C^{N+1}\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}}),

Hence for any
\gamma>0
, choosing
\widetilde{C}=C^{N+1}e^{\gamma N},
we have

(3.37)

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq\widetilde{C}e^{-\gamma t}\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}}).

While for
t>N\mathcal{T}
, one has
t=kN\mathcal{T}+\beta
, where
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
0\leq\beta<N\mathcal{T}
. By assumption,
\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}})\leq\alpha\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}})
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. So for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, by iteration,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+kN\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+kN\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}})\leq\alpha^{k}\rho_{r}(w_{1},{w_{2}}).

It then follows from inequality (
3.37
), (
3.38
) and the first part of Lemma
3.14
that

\displaystyle\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})

\displaystyle=\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s+\beta,s+\beta+kN\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s+\beta,s+\beta+kN\mathcal{T}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{k}\rho_{r}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq\alpha^{\frac{t-\beta}{N\mathcal{T}}}C^{N+1}\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})\leq Ce^{-\gamma t}\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}}),

for appropriate constants
C,\gamma>0
. The proof is then complete by invoking again the first part of Lemma
3.14
.
∎

The weak irreducibility, Lyapunov structure and gradient inequality give us the contractions on the state space at different scales. This fact is summarized in the following lemmas. Lemma
3.15
deals with those points that are far apart, where the contraction is guaranteed by the Lyapunov structure. The gradient inequality and Lyapunov structure give the contraction at small scales in Lemma
3.16
. And the contraction of the intermediate scale in Lemma
3.17
is given by the weak irreducibility and Lyapunov structure.

Under the conditions of Theorem
2.2
, the following three lemmas hold for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. The proof for the lemmas will be given in Appendix
6.4
since it is almost the same as that in
[
35
]
once we obtain the weak irreducibility, Lyapunov structure and the gradient inequality that are uniform in the initial time. Recall that the metric
d
is defined in (
3.35
), which depends on
\delta,\beta
and
r
.

Lemma 3.15
.

There is a constant
L>0
such that for any
\delta>0
,
\beta\in(0,1)
and
r\in[r_{0},1)
, there is
\alpha_{1}\in(0,1)
such that

\left.\begin{array}[]{l}\rho({w_{1}},{w_{2}})\geq L\\
\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})\geq\delta\end{array}\right\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\alpha_{1}d({w_{1}},{w_{2}}),

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 3.16
.

For any
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
there exist
n_{0}>0
, and
r\in[r_{0},1)
,
\delta>0
such that

\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})<\delta\Longrightarrow d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\alpha_{2}d({w_{1}},{w_{2}}),

for all
n>n_{0}
and
\beta\in(0,1)
.

Lemma 3.17
.

For any
L,\delta>0
,
r\in(0,1]
, there is some
n_{1}>0
such that for any
n>n_{1}
, there are
\beta,\alpha_{3}\in(0,1)
such that

\left.\begin{array}[]{l}\rho({w_{1}},{w_{2}})<L\\
\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})\geq\delta\end{array}\right\}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\alpha_{3}d({w_{1}},{w_{2}})

Now we prove Theorem
2.2
with the help of the above lemmas.

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

By Lemma
3.14
and the equivalence of the two metrics
\rho
and
d
, it suffices to show that

d(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})\leq\alpha d({w_{1}},{w_{2}}),

for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\alpha<1
and for every
({w_{1}},{w_{2}})\in H\times H
. By Lemma
3.16
, fixing an
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
, then there are
n_{0},r,\delta
such that for those
({w_{1}},{w_{2}})
with
\rho_{r}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})<\delta
, one has

d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\alpha_{2}d({w_{1}},{w_{2}})

for all
n>n_{0}
and
\beta\in(0,1).

Now fixing
L
as in Lemma
3.15
, then by Lemma
3.17
, for the fixed
L,\delta,r
, there is some
n_{1}
such that for
n>n_{1}
, there exist
\beta,\alpha_{3}
such that the implication in Lemma
3.17
holds true. Now for fixed
\delta,\beta,r,L
, there is
\alpha_{1}
such that the implication of Lemma
3.15
holds true. So the conclusion follows by taking
N>\max\{n_{0},n_{1}\}
and
\alpha=\max\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}\}<1.

∎

4.
Weak law of large numbers and central limit theorem

In this section, we prove limit theorems Theorem
2.3
for the solution process of the Navier-Stokes equation (
1.1
). The essential ingredients to obtain the results are the contraction (
2.6
) of the Markov transition operator
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}^{*}
on
\mathcal{P}(H)
, together with a Lyapunov structure. To the best of our knowledge, there are little known results of limit theorems for continuous time inhomogeneous Markov processes. Our first result is a weak law of large numbers for the continuous time inhomogeneous solution process which is proved in subsection
4.1
. However we are not able to prove a central limit theorem for the continuous time inhomogeneous solution process. In the theory of dynamical systems, when a system has a periodic structure, one usually consider its dynamics on the Poincare section. Therefore following the same idea, we turn to consider the restriction of the time inhomogeneous solution process to periodic times, which results in a discrete time homogeneous Markov chain. And in subsection (
4.2
) we show that both the weak law of large numbers and the central limit theorem hold for this Markov chain.

4.1.
Weak law of large numbers for the inhomogeneous solution process

In this subsection, we will prove the weak law of large numbers for the continuous time inhomogeneous solution process of Navier-Stokes equation (
1.1
). To be more specific, we will show the following theorem. Recall that
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{T}}=\mathbb{R}/\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}
, and we denote by
\lambda
the normalized Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume
G_{1}<1/c_{0}
,
A_{\infty}=H
. Then for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
w_{0}\in H
and
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
,

\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(w_{s,s+t}(w_{0}))dt=\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)

in probability.

Proof.

Let
Y_{T}=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(w_{s,s+t}(w_{0}))dt
and
v_{*}=\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)
. By Chebyshev’s inequality, to show Theorem
4.1
, it suffices to show that
\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{T}=v_{*}
and

\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{T}^{2}=v_{*}^{2}
. We first proof the theorem for bounded
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
. Note that by mixing property of the periodic invariant measure
\mu_{s}
in Theorem
2.1
and the Monge-Kantorovich daulity, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w_{0})dt-\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{s+t}(dw)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Lip_{\rho}}(\psi)\sup_{\mathrm{Lip_{\rho}}(\phi)\leq 1}\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w_{0})dt-\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{H}\phi(w)\mu_{s+t}(dw)dt\right|

(4.1)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Lip_{\rho}}(\psi)\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\rho(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\mu_{s})dt\leq C\mathrm{Lip_{\rho}}(\psi)\rho(\delta_{w_{0}},\mu_{s})\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\gamma t}dt\rightarrow 0\quad(T\rightarrow\infty).

For
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\nu_{\tau}=\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{\tau}(dw)
. By Theorem
3.2
, there is a constant
C>0
such that
\int_{H}\exp\left(2\kappa\eta\|w\|^{2}\right)\mu_{s}(dw)\leq C
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Note that
\rho(0,w)\leq\|w\|e^{\eta\|w\|^{2}}\leq Ce^{\kappa\eta\|w\|^{2}}
, so

|\nu_{\tau}|\leq|\psi(0)|+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\int_{H}\rho(0,w)\mu_{s}(dw)\leq|\psi(0)|+C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi).

The boundedness of
\nu_{\tau}
implies that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{s+t}(dw)dt=\frac{1}{T}\int_{s}^{s+T}\nu_{\tau}d\tau=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{s+T}\nu_{\tau}d\tau-\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{s}\nu_{\tau}d\tau\rightarrow v_{*}\quad(T\rightarrow\infty),

since

\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{s+T}\nu_{\tau}d\tau=\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{\tau}d\tau=\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\left(\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{k\mathcal{T}}\nu_{\tau}d\tau+\frac{1}{T}\int_{k\mathcal{T}}^{T}\nu_{\tau}d\tau\right)=v_{*}

by the representation
T=k\mathcal{T}+\beta
for unique
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in[0,\mathcal{T})
.
It follows from (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) that

(4.3)

\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{T}=\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w_{0})dt=v_{*}.

Note that the convergence (
4.3
) is valid for all
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
since we did not use boundedness of
\psi
in the proof.

By Markov property, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}Y_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{T^{2}}\mathbf{E}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\psi(w_{s,s+t}(w_{0}))\psi(w_{s,s+\tau}(w_{0}))d\tau dt

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\psi(w_{s,s+\tau}(w_{0}))\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi(w_{s,s+\tau}(w_{0}))d\tau dt

(4.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}\left(\psi\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi)(w_{0})\right)d\tau dt.

We first show that the convergence in (
4.3
) is uniform on any compact set, that is for any
\varepsilon>0
, and compact
K\subset H
, there is
T_{0}>0
such that for all
T\geq T_{0}
,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in K}\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w)dt-v_{*}\right|<\varepsilon.

Indeed,
\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w)dt
is uniformly bounded on
K
since
\psi
is bounded. And it is also equicontinuous since by the Monge-Kantorovich daulity

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w_{1})dt-\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w_{2})dt\right|\leq\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\rho(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}})dt

\displaystyle C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2})\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\gamma t}dt\leq C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2}).

So (
4.5
) follows by Arzela-Ascoli theorem. From the proof we see that the estimate (
4.5
) is also uniform with respect to the initial time
s
.

Since for any
w\in H
and bounded
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
, we have

\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\psi(w)dt=\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds),

hence the family of probability measures
\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w}dt
converges weakly to
\int_{Y}\mu_{s}\lambda(ds)
. Therefore
\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w}dt
is tight, that is for
\varepsilon>0
, there are compact subset
K
and
T_{1}>0
such that for
T\geq T_{1}
,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}^{*}\delta_{w}(K^{c})dt=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}(w,K^{c})dt<\varepsilon.

The proof of the following lemma will be given at the end of this subsection.

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
,
w_{0}\in H
, there is a
T_{1}>0
such that for
T\geq T_{1}
,

\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}\Big(\psi\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi-v_{*}\big)\Big)(w_{0})d\tau dt\right|<\varepsilon.

Observe that
\alpha(t):=\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+r}\psi(w_{0})dr\rightarrow v_{*}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
by (
4.3
). Hence
\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}t\alpha(t)dt=v_{*}
. Indeed, for
\varepsilon>0
, there is
T_{0}>0
such that for
t\geq T_{0}
,
|\alpha(t)-v_{*}|<\varepsilon
and
t|\alpha(t)-v_{*}|<M
for
t\in[0,T_{0}]
. Therefore for
T>T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}t\alpha(t)dt-v_{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}t\left|\alpha(t)-v_{*}\right|dt

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}t|\alpha(t)-v_{*}|dt+\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{T_{0}}^{T}t\varepsilon dt\leq\frac{MT_{0}^{2}}{T^{2}}+\frac{T^{2}-T_{0}^{2}}{T^{2}}\varepsilon<\varepsilon

by choosing large enough
T
.

Combining (
4.7
) with Lemma
4.2
, one has

\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}\left(\psi\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi)(w_{0})\right)d\tau dt

\displaystyle=\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}\left(\psi v_{*}\right)d\tau dt

\displaystyle=v_{*}\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}t\alpha(t)dt=v_{*}^{2}.

In view of (
4.4
), we see that
\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{T}^{2}=v_{*}^{2}
, which completes the proof of Theorem
4.1
for bounded Lipschitz observables.

Now for
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
, we define for
L>1

\displaystyle\psi_{L}(w):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\psi(w),&\text{ when }|\psi(w)|\leq L,\\
L,&\text{ when }\psi(w)>L,\\
-L,&\text{ when }\psi(w)<-L.\end{array}\right.

set
v_{*}^{L}=\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi_{L}(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)
,
\psi^{L}=|\psi-\psi_{L}|
.
Again by Theorem
3.2
, there is a constant
C>0
such that
\int_{H}\exp\left(2\kappa\eta\|w\|^{2}\right)\mu_{s}(dw)\leq C
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Note that
\rho(0,w)\leq\|w\|e^{\eta\|w\|^{2}}\leq Ce^{\kappa\eta\|w\|^{2}}
, so

\int_{Y}\int_{H}|\psi(w)|\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)\leq|\psi(0)|+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\int_{Y}\int_{H}\rho(0,w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)\leq|\psi(0)|+C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi).

Hence by the dominated convergence theorem,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi^{L}(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)=0.

Since
\psi_{L}
is bounded, by Theorem
4.1
for bounded Lipschitz observables that we have proved, one has

(4.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi_{L}(w_{s,s+t}(w_{0}))dt=v_{*}^{L}.

By combining (
4.11
) with (
4.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi^{L}(w_{s.s+t})dt\leq

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{E}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi^{L}(w_{s.s+t})dt-\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi^{L}(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)\right|

\displaystyle+\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi^{L}(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds)\rightarrow 0\quad(T\rightarrow\infty,L\rightarrow\infty).

Therefore by Markov inequality,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi^{L}(w_{s.s+t})dt\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in probability}.

Now observe that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(w_{s,s+t})dt-v_{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(w_{s,s+t})dt-\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi_{L}(w_{s,s+t})dt\right|+\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi_{L}(w_{s,s+t})dt-v_{*}^{L}\right|+|v_{*}^{L}-v_{*}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+t})dt+\left|\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi_{L}(w_{s,s+t})dt-v_{*}^{L}\right|+\int_{Y}\int_{H}\psi^{L}(w)\mu_{s}(dw)\lambda(ds),

whence
\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\psi(w_{s,s+t})dt=v_{*}
in probability by (
4.11
)-(
4.13
). The proof of Theorem
4.1
for any Lipschitz observable is then completed. It remains to supply a proof for Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Let
K
be the compact set and
T_{1}>0
as in (
4.6
). Note

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}\Big(\psi\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi-v_{*}\big)\Big)(w_{0})d\tau dt\right|\leq K_{1}+K_{2},

where

K_{1}=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{H}\Big(\psi\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi-v_{*}\big)\Big)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)d\tau dt\right|,

K_{2}=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{H}\Big(\psi\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi-v_{*}\big)\Big)\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}(w)\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)d\tau dt\right|.

We first estimate
K_{1}
. For the compact set
K
, choose
T_{0}>T_{1}
as in (
4.5
), then for
T>T_{0}

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\tau}^{T}\int_{H}\Big(\psi\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+t}\psi-v_{*}\big)\Big)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)dtd\tau\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}(T-\tau)\int_{H}\psi(w)\left(\frac{1}{T-\tau}\int_{0}^{T-\tau}\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+\tau,s+\tau+r}\psi-v_{*}\big)(w)\right)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)dr\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)d\tau\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}t\int_{H}\psi(w)\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+T-t,s+T-t+r}\psi-v_{*}\big)(w)\right)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)dr\mathcal{P}_{s,s+T-t}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq K_{11}+K_{12},

where

\displaystyle K_{11}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}t\int_{H}\psi(w)\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+T-t,s+T-t+r}\psi-v_{*}\big)(w)\right)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)dr\mathcal{P}_{s,s+T-t}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}t\|\psi\|_{\infty}\big(\|\psi\|_{\infty}+v_{*}\big)d\tau\leq 2\left(\frac{T_{0}}{T}\right)^{2}\|\psi\|_{\infty}\big(\|\psi\|_{\infty}+v_{*}\big),

And

\displaystyle K_{12}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{T^{2}}\int_{T_{0}}^{T}t\int_{H}\psi(w)\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathcal{P}_{s+T-t,s+T-t+r}\psi-v_{*}\big)(w)\right)\mathbb{I}_{K}(w)dr\mathcal{P}_{s,s+T-t}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T^{2}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}\int_{T_{0}}^{T}t\sup_{w\in K}\left|\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s+T-t,s+T-t+r}\psi(w)dr-v_{*}\right|dt\leq\varepsilon\|\psi\|_{\infty}.

For
K_{2}
, we apply (
4.6
),

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{4}{T^{2}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{H}\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}(w)\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(dw)d\tau dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{T^{2}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{T_{0}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}(w_{0},{K^{c}})d\tau dt+\int_{T_{0}}^{T}t\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\tau}(w_{0},{K^{c}})d\tau\right)dt\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{T^{2}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}T_{0}^{2}+\frac{T^{2}}{2}\varepsilon\right).

Combining the above estimates and choosing large enough
T
, we then arrive at Lemma
4.2
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
4.1
is then completed.
∎

Remark 4.1
.

Note that the convergence (
4.3
) is actually a special corollary of the Von Neumann Theorem (formula 6.28) mentioned in
[
19
]
.

4.2.
Limit theorems for the restriction of the inhomogeneous solution process
to periodic times

In this subsection, we will prove the weak law of large numbers and central limit theorem for the time homogeneous Markov chain obtained by restricting the solution process starting at
s\in\mathbb{R}
at periodic times
\{s+n\mathcal{T}\}_{n\geq 0}
.

For
w_{0}\in H,s\in\mathbb{R}
, recall that
w_{s,t}(w_{0})=w(t,\omega;s,w_{0})
is the solution of system (
1.1
) starting from
w_{0}
at time
s
. Let
\{\mu_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
be the unique periodic invariant measure of the transition operator
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}
. For integer
k\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{F}_{k}=\mathcal{F}_{s+k\mathcal{T}}
. For
0\leq i\leq j
, and any Borel set
A\subset H
, we have transition probabilities
\mathfrak{P}_{i,j}(w_{0},A):=\mathcal{P}_{s+i\mathcal{T},s+j\mathcal{T}}(w_{0},A)=\mathbf{P}(w(s+j\mathcal{T};s+i\mathcal{T},w_{0})\in A)
of the Markov chain
\{w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\}_{k\geq 0}
such that

(1)

\mathbf{P}(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\in A|\mathfrak{F}_{i})=\mathfrak{P}_{i,j}(w_{s,s+i\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}),A)
,
\mathfrak{P}_{i,j}(w_{0},A)=\delta_{w_{0}}(A)
;

(2)

It is time homogeneous, i.e.
\mathfrak{P}_{i,j}=\mathfrak{P}_{0,j-i}
since
\mathcal{P}_{s+i\mathcal{T},s+j\mathcal{T}}=\mathcal{P}_{s,s+(j-i)\mathcal{T}}
by periodicity.

Let
\mathfrak{P}_{k}=\mathcal{P}_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}
. Then
\mathfrak{P}_{k}
is a Markov semigroup and
\mu_{s}
is the unique invariant measure. What’s more, by Theorem
3.2
, one has
\rho(\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\mu_{s})\leq Ce^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}\rho(\mu,\mu_{s})
for any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(H)
.

The main result of this subsection is the following limit theorem for the Markov chain
\{w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}\}_{k\geq 0}.
We also denote the integration of a function
\psi
with respect to the probability measure
\mu
by
\langle\mu,\psi\rangle=\int_{H}\psi(z)\mu(dz)
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Assume
G_{1}<1/c_{0}
,
A_{\infty}=H
. Then for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
w_{0}\in H
and any Lipschitz function
\psi:H\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have the following:

(1)

Weak law of large numbers (WLLN),

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)=\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle

in probability.

(2)

Let
\widetilde{\psi}_{s}(w_{0}):=\psi(w_{0})-\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle
. Then the central limit theorem (CLT) holds:

(4.15)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\widetilde{\psi}_{s}\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)<\xi\right)=\Phi_{\sigma}(\xi),\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R},

where
\Phi_{\sigma}(\cdot)
is the distribution function of a normal random variable with variance
\sigma^{2}
, and

\sigma^{2}=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\widetilde{\psi}_{s}\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)\right]^{2},

where
\sigma:=\sigma(s)
is
\mathcal{T}
-periodic in
s
.

4.2.1.
Proof of WLLN

Let
Y_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)
and
v_{s}=\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle
. Then by Chebyshev’s inequality, it suffices to show that
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{N}=v_{s}
and
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}Y_{N}^{2}=v_{s}^{2}
. Assume first that
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
is bounded. Then

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}Y_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w_{0})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}},\psi\rangle\rightarrow v_{s},

since the average
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}
converges to
\mu_{s}
in the Wasserstein metric. Indeed, for any
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
with
\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\leq 1
, and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(H)
, by Theorem
3.2
, the invariance of
\mu_{s}
, and the Monge-Kantorovich daulity, one has

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\psi\rangle-\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\psi\rangle-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu_{s},\psi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\left|\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\psi\rangle-\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu_{s},\psi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Ce^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}\rho(\mu,\mu_{s})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\rho(\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu_{s})\leq\frac{C}{N(1-e^{-\gamma\mathcal{T}})}\rho(\mu,\mu_{s}).

In addition, the convergence (
4.16
) is uniform on any compact set by Arzelà–Ascoli theorem since
\psi_{N}(w):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w)
is uniformly bounded by boundedness of
\psi
and it is equicontinuous by noting that

\displaystyle|\psi_{N}(w_{1})-\psi_{N}(w_{2})|\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\left|\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w_{1})-\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Ce^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2})

\displaystyle\leq C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2}).

Hence for any
\varepsilon>0
and compact set
K\subset H
, there is some
N_{0}
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
, one has

(4.17)

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in K}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w)-v_{s}\right|<\varepsilon.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}Y_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathbf{E}\left[\psi\left(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)\psi\left(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)\right]-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}\left[\psi(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathbf{E}\left[\psi\left(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi\left(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)\right]-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{m}\psi^{2}(w_{0})

(4.18)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\psi\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi)(w_{0})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{m}\psi^{2}(w_{0}).

The second term on the last equality (
4.18
) tends to
0
since
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{m}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}
converges to
\mu_{s}
weakly. To estimate the first term, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there is
N_{0}>0
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|<\varepsilon.

The proof of this lemma will be given at the end of this subsection.
Note that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\psi(w_{0})=v_{s}.

Indeed,

\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\psi(w_{0})=\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}m\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\psi(w_{0})\right),

and
\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\psi(w_{0})=v_{s}.
Therefore, if we set
\alpha_{m}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\psi(w_{0})
, then for any
\varepsilon>0,
there is some
M_{0}>0
such that for any
m\geq M_{0}
,
|\alpha_{m}-v_{s}|<\varepsilon
. Hence

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}m\alpha_{m}-v_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}m|\alpha_{m}-v_{s}|+\frac{|v_{s}|}{N}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{M_{0}-1}m|\alpha_{m}-v_{s}|+\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=M_{0}}^{N-1}m\varepsilon+\frac{|v_{s}|}{N}<\varepsilon,

by taking
N
large enough. Combining (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) with Lemma
4.4
, one has the equality (
4.14
) for bounded Lipschitz observables. For unbounded Lipschitz function
\psi
, define
\psi_{L}
as (
4.1
).
Let
v_{s}^{L}=\langle\mu_{s},\psi_{L}\rangle
and
\psi^{L}=|\psi-\psi_{L}|
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)-v_{s}\right|

(4.20)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))+\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi_{L}\left(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0})\right)-v_{s}^{L}\right|+\left|v_{s}^{L}-v_{s}\right|.

Since
\mu_{s}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(H)
, we have
\langle\mu_{s},|\psi|\rangle\leq\langle\mu_{s},|\psi(0)|+\rho(0,w)\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rangle<\infty
. So
\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\left|v_{s}^{L}-v_{s}\right|=0
by the dominated convergence theorem. Also the second term of (
4.20
) converges to
0
in probability by the already proved (
4.14
) for bounded Lipschitz observables. It follows from Theorem
3.2
, the invariance of
\mu_{s}
, and the Monge-Kantorovich daulity that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{E}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))-\langle\mu_{s},\psi^{L}\rangle\right|+\langle\mu_{s},\psi^{L}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\left|\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}},\psi^{L}\rangle-\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu_{s},\psi^{L}\rangle\right|+\langle\mu_{s},\psi^{L}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Ce^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(\delta_{w_{0}},\mu_{s})+\langle\mu_{s},\psi^{L}\rangle.

Hence

\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))=0.

As a result, by Markov inequality,
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi^{L}(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}}(w_{0}))
converges to
0
in probability as
N\rightarrow\infty
and
L\rightarrow\infty
. Combining this with (
4.20
), we obtain (
4.14
) for unbounded Lipschitz observables. Now we supply a proof for Lemma
4.4
.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

By mixing property of
\mu_{s}
,
\rho(\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu,\mu_{s})\leq Ce^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}\rho(\mu,\mu_{s})
, hence the family of probability measures
\{\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}\}_{k\geq 0}
is tight. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there is a compact set
K
and
N_{0}>0
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{0}}(K^{c})<\varepsilon.

Then choose
N_{0}^{*}\geq N_{0}
as in (
4.17
) for given
\varepsilon
and
K
. Now

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{K}\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|+\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|

\displaystyle:=S_{1}+S_{2}.

Note that

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m=n}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{K}\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m-n}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|=\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1-n}\mathfrak{P}_{n}(\mathbb{I}_{K}\psi(\mathfrak{P}_{m}\psi-v_{s}))(w_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{0}^{*}-1}(N-n)\mathfrak{P}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{K}\psi\left(\frac{1}{N-n}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1-n}\mathfrak{P}_{m}\psi-v_{s}\right)\right)(w_{0})\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=N_{0}^{*}}^{N-1}(N-n)\mathfrak{P}_{n}\left(\mathbb{I}_{K}\psi\left(\frac{1}{N-n}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1-n}\mathfrak{P}_{m}\psi-v_{s}\right)\right)\left(w_{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2N_{0}^{*}}{N}\|\psi\|_{\infty}(\|\psi\|_{\infty}+|v_{s}|)+C\|\psi\|_{\infty}\varepsilon\leq C\|\psi\|_{\infty}\varepsilon,

for large
N
. And

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N^{2}}\sum_{m=0}^{N-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}(w_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{N_{0}^{*}-1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}(w_{0})+\sum_{m=N_{0}^{*}}^{N-1}\frac{m+1}{m+1}\sum_{n=0}^{m}\mathfrak{P}_{n}\mathbb{I}_{K^{c}}(w_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\psi\|_{\infty}^{2}\varepsilon,

for
N
large enough. The proof is then complete.
∎

4.2.2.
Proof of CLT

Throughout this subsection, we denote by
B_{R}(0)=\{w\in H:\rho(0,w)<R\}
the ball under the metric
\rho
. Denote the initial condition as
w
. And denote
\mathbf{E}[X,Y\geq Z]=\mathbf{E}X\mathbb{I}_{\{Y\geq Z\}}
for given random variables
X,Y,Z
. The following estimate shows a Lyapunov structure of system (
1.1
), which will be useful in the proof.

Lemma 4.5
.

There is
\delta>0
so that for all
R>0
, there is constant
C=C(R,\delta)
independent of initial time
s
such that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+t}(w))^{2+\delta}\leq C.

Proof.

Recall that
V(w)=e^{\eta\|w\|^{2}}
, and
\kappa>1
as in Proposition
6.3
. From the definition of the metric
\rho
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+t}(w))^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\|w_{s,s+t}(w)\|^{2+\delta}V^{2+\delta}(w_{s,s+t}(w))

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbf{E}V^{\kappa(2+\delta)}(w_{s,s+t}(w))\leq C\exp(\kappa(2+\delta)\eta\|w\|^{2}),

by taking
\eta
small so that
\kappa(2+\delta)\eta\leq\eta_{0}
and one can apply the estimate (
6.1
) in the last step. The proof is complete.
∎

The proof of (
4.15
) is based on the central limit theorem for martingales proved in
[
44
]
by a martingale approximation approach, where the idea dates back to the early works of Gordin
[
31
]
, Kipnis and Varadhan
[
38
]
. Suppose that
\left\{\mathfrak{F}_{n},n\geq 0\right\}
is a filtration over
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbf{P})
such that
\mathfrak{F}_{0}
is trivial and
\left\{Z_{n},n\geq 1\right\}
is a sequence of square integrable martingale differences, i.e. it is
\left\{\mathfrak{F}_{n},n\geq 1\right\}
adapted,
\mathbf{E}Z_{n}^{2}<+\infty
and
\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{n}\,\middle|\,\mathfrak{F}_{n-1}\right]=0
for all
n\geq 1.
Define also the martingale

M_{N}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}Z_{j},\quad N\geq 1,\quad M_{0}:=0

Its quadratic variation equals
[M]_{N}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathfrak{F}_{j-1}\right]
for
N\geq 1.
Assume also that:

(M1) for every
\varepsilon>0,

\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j+1}^{2},\left|Z_{j+1}\right|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}\right]=0,

(M2) the condition

\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}Z_{n}^{2}<+\infty

and there exists
\sigma\geq 0
such that

\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\mathbf{E}\left|\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{E}\left[[M]_{mK}-[M]_{(m-1)K}\,\middle|\,\mathfrak{F}_{(m-1)K}\right]-\sigma^{2}\right|=0

and

(M3) for every
\varepsilon>0

\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\ell K}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=(m-1)K}^{mK-1}\mathbf{E}\left[1+Z_{j+1}^{2},\left|M_{j}-M_{(m-1)K}\right|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}\right]=0.

Then one has

Theorem 4.6
.

[
44
]

Under conditions (M1)-(M3), one has

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}[M]_{N}}{N}=\sigma^{2}

and

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}e^{i\theta M_{N}/\sqrt{N}}=e^{-\sigma^{2}\theta^{2}/2},\quad\forall\theta\in\mathbb{R}.

Assume
\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
and
\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle=0
, otherwise one can replace
\psi
by
\psi-\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle
. In this subsection,
\left\{\mathfrak{F}_{n},n\geq 0\right\}
denotes the natural filtration generated by the Markov chain
\{w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w)\}_{n\geq 0}
. Let
\alpha_{n}(w)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w)
. We have

Lemma 4.7
.

The sequence of functions
\alpha_{n}
converges uniformly on bounded sets. The point wise limit
\alpha:=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\alpha_{n}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
and
for any
n\geq m
,

\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}\right]=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{N}(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}\right].

Proof.

By
\langle\mu_{s},\psi\rangle=0
, Theorem
3.2
, the invariance of
\mu_{s}
, and the Monge-Kantorovich daulity, we have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{n}(w)-\alpha_{m}(w)|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{k=m}^{n}\left(\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi(w)-\langle\mu_{s},\mathfrak{P}_{k}\psi\rangle\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=m}^{n}\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w},\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\mu_{s})\leq C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(\delta_{w},\mu_{s})\sum_{k=m}^{n}e^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}}<\varepsilon

for
n>m>N
and
N
large enough. Hence
\alpha_{n}
converges uniformly on bounded sets.
Note that

|\alpha_{N}(w_{1})-\alpha_{N}(w_{2})|\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N}|\langle\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{1}}-\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w_{2}},\psi\rangle|\leq C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2})\sum_{k=0}^{N}e^{-\gamma k\mathcal{T}},

hence
|\alpha(w_{1})-\alpha(w_{2})|\leq C\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w_{1},w_{2})
, where
C
does not depend on
w_{1},w_{2}\in H
. So
\alpha\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H).
Since each
\alpha_{N}
and
\alpha
are Lipschitz continuous and
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(H)
, so they are
\mathfrak{P}_{k}^{*}\delta_{w}
integrable. By the dominated convergence theorem, one has for any
w\in H
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}\alpha_{N}(w)=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{N}(w_{s,s+(n-m)\mathcal{T}}(w))\right]=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}^{*}\delta_{w},\alpha_{N}\rangle=\langle\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}^{*}\delta_{w},\alpha\rangle=\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}\alpha(w).

Hence

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha_{N}(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}\right]=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}\alpha_{N}(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})=\mathfrak{P}_{n-m}\alpha(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})=\mathbf{E}\left[\alpha(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}\right].

∎

To apply Theorem
4.6
, we decompose the average in (
4.15
) into the sum of a martingale and a small remainder term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}M_{N}+R_{N},

where
M_{N}=\alpha({w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}})-\alpha(w)+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})
,
M_{0}=0
and
R_{N}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\alpha(w)-\alpha({w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}}))
.

Proposition 4.8
.

\{M_{N}\}_{N\geq 0}
is a martingale with respect to the natural filtration
\{\mathfrak{F}_{N}\}_{N\geq 0}
.

Proof.

First note that
|\alpha(w)|\leq|\alpha(w)-\alpha(0)|+|\alpha(0)|\leq C(1+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(w,0)).
Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}|M_{N}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}|\alpha({w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}})|+\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w)|+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}|\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}))+|\alpha(w)|+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathfrak{P}_{k}|\psi|(w)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}))+|\alpha(w)|+C(1+\rho(w,0))<\infty.

Also for
m\leq N
,

\mathbf{E}[M_{N}|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]=\mathbf{E}[\alpha({w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]-\alpha(w)+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\mathbf{E}[\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]+\sum_{k=m}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}[\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]

and

\displaystyle\sum_{k=m}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}[\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}\mathbf{E}[\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]-\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\mathbf{E}[\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}\mathfrak{P}_{k-m}\psi(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})-\sum_{k=N}^{\infty}\mathfrak{P}_{k-N}\mathfrak{P}_{N-m}\psi(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle=\alpha(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})-\mathfrak{P}_{N-m}\alpha(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle=\alpha(w_{s,s+m\mathcal{T}})-\mathbf{E}[\alpha(w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}})|\mathfrak{F}_{m}].

Therefore
\mathbf{E}[M_{N}|\mathfrak{F}_{m}]=M_{m}.

∎

Lemma 4.9
.

\mathbf{E}R_{N}\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

We have by Theorem
3.2
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w_{s,s+N\mathcal{T}})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left|\langle\mathfrak{P}_{N}^{*}\delta_{w}-\mathfrak{P}_{N}^{*}\mu_{s},|\alpha|\rangle\right|+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\langle\mathfrak{P}_{N}^{*}\mu_{s},|\alpha|\rangle.

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(|\alpha|)e^{-\gamma N\mathcal{T}}\rho(\delta_{w},\mu_{s})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\langle\mu_{s},|\alpha|\rangle\rightarrow 0\quad(N\rightarrow\infty).

∎

Setting
Z_{n}=M_{n}-M_{n-1}
for
n\geq 1
, we will verify the conditions of Theorem
4.6
to finish the proof of (
4.15
).

Verification of condition (M1).

Set
F(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{1}^{2}(w)\mathbb{I}_{|Z_{1}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}}\right]
. Note that by the periodicity one has for each
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle F(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(\alpha({w_{s+k\mathcal{T},s+(k+1)\mathcal{T}}})-\alpha(w)+\psi(w)\right)^{2},\left|\alpha({w_{s+k\mathcal{T},s+(k+1)\mathcal{T}}})-\alpha(w)+\psi(w)\right|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}\right].

Then by the Markov property,

\mathfrak{P}_{k-1}F(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[F(w_{s,s+(k-1)\mathcal{T}}(w))\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{k}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|Z_{k}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}}|\mathfrak{F}_{k-1}\right]\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{k}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|Z_{k}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}}\right]

∎

Hence

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j+1}^{2},\left|Z_{j+1}\right|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}\right]=\left\langle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathfrak{P}_{j-1}^{*}\delta_{w},F\right\rangle.

We recall a lemma from
[
44
]
.

Lemma 4.10
.

[
44
]

If
\mu_{N}
converges to
\mu
weakly, and
G_{N}\rightarrow 0
uniformly on compact sets and there is
\eta>0
such that
\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle\mu_{N},|G_{N}|^{1+\eta}\rangle<\infty
, then
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle\mu_{N},G_{N}\rangle=0
.

Note that
\nu_{N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathfrak{P}_{j-1}^{*}\delta_{w}
converges to
\mu_{s}
weakly. To verify condition (M1), it remains to show that
F_{N}(w)=F(w)
converges to
0
uniformly on compact sets and there is some
\eta>0
that meets the condition of the above Lemma
4.10
.
Note that

\displaystyle F_{N}(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{1}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|Z_{1}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\mathbf{P}(|Z_{1}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N})^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}}{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}N^{\frac{2+\delta}{2}}}\right)^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}}=\frac{\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}}{\varepsilon^{\delta}N^{\delta/2}}.

And

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(w))-\alpha(w)+\psi(w)|^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(w))-\alpha(w)|^{2+\delta}+|\psi(w)|^{2+\delta}\right)

(4.21)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)^{2+\delta}\mathbf{E}\rho(w_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(w),w)^{2+\delta}+|\psi(0)|^{2+\delta}+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)^{2+\delta}\rho(0,w)^{2+\delta}\right).

Therefore for any
R>0
, by Lemma
4.5
, one has

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}F_{N}(w)=\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{1}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|Z_{1}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{N}}\right]\leq\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\delta}N^{\delta/2}}\rightarrow 0\quad(N\rightarrow\infty).

Hence
F_{N}(w)
converges to
0
uniformly on any compact set. By (
4.21
),

\displaystyle\langle\nu_{N},|F_{N}|^{1+\delta/2}\rangle\leq\left\langle\nu_{N},\mathbf{E}|Z_{1}|^{2+\delta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)^{2+\delta}\int_{H}\mathbf{E}\rho(w_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(z),z)^{2+\delta}\nu_{N}(dz)+|\psi(0)|^{2+\delta}+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)^{2+\delta}\int_{H}\rho(0,z)^{2+\delta}\nu_{N}(dz)\right),

where by Lemma
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{H}\rho(0,z)^{2+\delta}\nu_{N}(dz)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathfrak{P}_{j-1}\rho(0,w)^{2+\delta}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+(j-1)\mathcal{T}}(w))^{2+\delta}<\infty,

and

\displaystyle\int_{H}\mathbf{E}\rho(w_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(z),0)^{2+\delta}\nu_{N}(dz)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{H}\mathfrak{P}_{1}\rho(0,z)^{2+\delta}\mathfrak{P}_{j-1}^{*}\delta_{w}(dz)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathfrak{P}_{j}\rho(0,w)^{2+\delta}<\infty.

Hence
\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle\nu_{N},|F_{N}|^{1+\delta/2}\rangle<\infty
. The condition
(M1)
then follows from Lemma
4.10
.

Verification of condition (M2).

We first show that
Z_{n}
is a square integrable martingale. Indeed, Lemma
4.5
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}Z_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})-\alpha(w_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}})|^{2}+\mathbf{E}|\psi(w_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}})|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbf{E}\left[\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)^{2}\left(\rho(0,w_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})^{2}+\rho(0,w_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}})^{2}\right)+|\psi(0)|^{2}+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)^{2}\rho(0,w_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle<\infty.

By periodicity and Markov property, for any
\sigma\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\mathbf{E}\left|\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{E}\left[[M]_{mK}-[M]_{(m-1)K}\,\middle|\,\mathfrak{F}_{(m-1)K}\right]-\sigma^{2}\right|=\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\mathfrak{P}_{(m-1)K}|H_{K}|(w),

where
H_{K}(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[\frac{1}{K}[M]_{K}-\sigma^{2}\right]=\mathbf{E}\left[\frac{1}{K}M_{K}^{2}-\sigma^{2}\right]
.
Following the same strategy as the proof of Lemma 5.5 in
[
44
]
, one can show that
H_{K}\in C(H)
and

\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\left\langle\mathfrak{P}_{(m-1)K}^{*}\delta_{w},|H_{K}|\right\rangle<\infty.

Hence
\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\left\langle\mathfrak{P}_{(m-1)K}^{*}\delta_{w},|H_{K}|\right\rangle=\langle\mu_{s},|H_{K}|\rangle.
Note that
H_{K}(w)=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\mathfrak{P}_{j}J(w)
, where
J=\mathbf{E}M_{1}^{2}-\sigma^{2}
. And
\langle\mu_{s},|H_{K}|\rangle=\mathbf{E}\left|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\mathfrak{P}_{j}J(X_{0})\right|
, and
X_{0}
is an initial data with law
\mu_{s}
. Since
\mu_{s}
is ergodic under the Markov semigroup
\{\mathfrak{P}_{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
, by the Birkhoff ergodic theorem, one has for the stationary Markov chain
X_{j}:=w(s+j\mathcal{T},\omega;s,X_{0})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\mathfrak{P}_{j}(\mathbf{E}M_{1}^{2})(X_{0})=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}(\mathbf{E}M_{1}^{2})(X_{j})\rightarrow\int_{H}(\mathbf{E}M_{1}^{2})(z)\mu_{s}(dz),\quad\mathbf{P}\text{-a.s.}\quad(K\rightarrow\infty).

Hence if we choose
\sigma^{2}=\langle\mu_{s},\mathbf{E}M_{1}^{2}\rangle
, then
\langle\mu_{s},|H_{K}|\rangle\rightarrow 0
as
K\rightarrow\infty
, which completes the proof of condition (M2).
∎

Verification of condition (M3).

By the periodicity and Markov property, we can rewrite the expression in condition (M3) as

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=(m-1)K}^{mK-1}\mathbf{E}\left[1+Z_{j+1}^{2},\left|M_{j}-M_{(m-1)K}\right|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}\right]=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\left\langle Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w},G_{\ell,j}\right\rangle,

where
Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w}=\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell}\mathfrak{P}_{(m-1)K}^{*}\delta_{w}
and
G_{\ell,j}(w)=\mathbf{E}\left[\left(1+Z_{j+1}^{2}\right)\mathbb{I}_{|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\right].
To show that the limit in the condition (M3) vanishes, it suffices to prove that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\left\langle Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w},G_{\ell,j}\right\rangle=0,\text{ for }j=0,1,\cdots,K-1.

Let
\widetilde{\alpha}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{P}_{k}|\psi|
, then
\widetilde{\alpha}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(H)
as in Lemma
4.7
. Noting that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}|M_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}|\alpha(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})-\alpha(w)|+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{E}|\psi(w_{s,s+k\mathcal{T}})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)\mathbf{E}\rho(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}},w)+\widetilde{\alpha}(w)\leq\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)\mathbf{E}\rho(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}},w)+\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\widetilde{\alpha})\rho(w,0)+\widetilde{\alpha}(0).

Therefore by Markov inequality and Lemma
4.5
, there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{P}(|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K})\leq\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\frac{\mathbf{E}|M_{j}|}{\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}.

Observe that

|\alpha(w_{s,s+(j+1)\mathcal{T}})-\alpha(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})|\leq\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\alpha)\left(\rho(0,w_{s,s+(j+1)\mathcal{T}})+\rho(0,w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})\right),

|\psi(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})|\leq\mathrm{Lip}_{\rho}(\psi)\rho(0,w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})+|\psi(0)|.

Therefore

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}\left[Z_{j+1}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\left(\mathbf{E}\left[|\alpha(w_{s,s+(j+1)\mathcal{T}})-\alpha(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})|^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\right]+\mathbf{E}\left[\psi(w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{0\leq j\leq K-1}\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}\left[\rho(0,w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})^{2}\mathbb{I}_{|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{0\leq j\leq K-1}\left(\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})^{2+\delta}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+\delta}}\left(\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}\mathbf{P}\left(|M_{j}|\geq\varepsilon\sqrt{\ell K}\right)\right)^{\frac{\delta}{2+\delta}},

where the above constant does not depend on
\ell
. Hence for any
R>0
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{w\in B_{R}(0)}G_{\ell,j}=0.

Note that

\displaystyle\left\langle Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w},G_{\ell,j}^{1+\frac{2}{\delta}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\int_{H}\mathbf{E}(1+Z_{j+1}^{2}(z))^{1+\frac{\delta}{2}}Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w}(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\langle Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w},\mathbf{E}Z_{j+1}^{2+\delta}\rangle)\leq C(1+\sup_{j\geq 0}\mathbf{E}\rho(0,w_{s,s+j\mathcal{T}})^{2+\delta}).

Again by Lemma
4.5
,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\left\langle Q_{\ell,K}^{*}\delta_{w},G_{\ell,j}^{1+\frac{2}{\delta}}\right\rangle<\infty.

The conclusion (
4.22
) then follows from (
4.23
), (
4.24
) and Lemma
4.10
, which in turn completes the verification of condition (M3).
∎

5.
Larger viscosity implies a trivial periodic attractor

We have shown that for periodic force
f(t)
, if
G_{1}<1/c_{0}
, then the system (
1.1
) has a unique periodic invariant measure. The aim of this section is to show that if the viscosity is larger, then the dynamics is actually trivial in the sense that the unique invariant measure is supported on a unique exponentially stable random periodic solution. We take the approach from
[
45
]
, where the case when
f=0
has been proved.

For
\alpha\in(0,1]
, let
\delta_{0}:=\nu-\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}}\left(\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\alpha\nu}+\mathcal{B}_{0}\right)
. We will prove the following result in this section.

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume
\delta_{0}>0
. Then there exists a random
\mathcal{T}
-periodic solution
w^{*}(t,x)
of equation (
1.1
) in the sense of the definition given by equation (
2.1
). Moreover,
w^{*}(t,x)
attracts all other solutions both in forward and pullback times. The law of
w^{*}(t,x)
gives the unique
\mathcal{T}
-periodic invariant measure.

In particular, when
\alpha=1
, the condition
\delta_{0}>0
is equivalent to the condition
G_{2}<1/c_{0}
, so that we obtain Theorem
2.4
from Theorem
5.1
.
The proof of Theorem
5.1
is a combination of Proposition
5.2
and Theorem
5.3
below.

We begin by comparing any two solutions that start from different initial data. Let
w_{0},\widetilde{w}_{0}\in H
, and
\mathfrak{e}_{t}=w_{s,t}(w_{0})-\widetilde{w}_{s,t}(\widetilde{w}_{0})
. In view of equation (
1.1
), we see that
\mathfrak{e}_{t}
solves the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathfrak{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}\mathfrak{e}_{t}-B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t},w_{s,t})+B(\mathcal{K}\widetilde{w}_{s,t},\widetilde{w}_{s,t})

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}\mathfrak{e}_{t}-B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t},w_{s,t})+B(\mathcal{K}(w_{s,t}-\mathfrak{e}_{t}),w_{s,t}-\mathfrak{e}_{t})

\displaystyle=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}\mathfrak{e}_{t}-B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t},\mathfrak{e}_{t})+B(\mathcal{K}\mathfrak{e}_{t},w_{s,t})+B(\mathcal{K}\mathfrak{e}_{t},\mathfrak{e}_{t}).

It then follows from
\langle B(\mathcal{K}w,v),v\rangle=0
and basic estimates of the nonlinear term (see Proposition 6.1 in
[
9
]
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\langle\mathfrak{e}_{t},\partial_{t}\mathfrak{e}_{t}\rangle=-2\nu\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+2\langle B(\mathcal{K}\mathfrak{e}_{t},w_{s,t}),\mathfrak{e}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-2\nu\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+2c_{0}\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|_{1}\leq-\nu\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{\nu}\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|^{2}\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}^{2}.

Hence by the Gronwall’s inequality,

\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathfrak{e}_{s}\|^{2}\exp\left(-\nu(t-s)+\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{\nu}\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr\right).

From equation (
6.7
) one obtains that for any
\alpha\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{\nu}\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr\leq\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}}\left(\|w_{0}\|^{2}+M(s,t)+(t-s)\left(\frac{\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\alpha\nu}+\mathcal{B}_{0}\right)\right),

where
M(s,t)=2\int_{s}^{t}\langle w_{s,r},GdW_{r}\rangle
. As a result,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{e}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathfrak{e}_{s}\|^{2}e^{-\left(t-s\right)\left(\delta_{0}-\mathrm{\Gamma}(s,t)\right)},

where
\mathrm{\Gamma}(s,t)=\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}(t-s)}\left(\|w_{0}\|^{2}+M(s,t)\right)
. Just as in
[
45
]
, it turns out that the average
\mathrm{\Gamma}(s,t)
can be sufficiently small for fixed
s
and large (random)
t
. Hence as long as
\delta_{0}>0
, or equivalently if
\nu
is large, then the dissipation dominates so that the dynamics is contracting in the state space. In what follows, the inequality (
5.1
) will be used to construct the unique random periodic solution that attracts all other solutions. The virtue of the proof is based on controlling the term
\mathrm{\Gamma}(s,t)
. Throughout the proof, we fix
\alpha\in(0,1]
.

The following proposition allows us to construct a solution that starts from
-\infty
. Since the proof is essentially the same as that in
[
46
]
, we omit it here.

Proposition 5.2
.

Fix a
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
and
t_{1}\in\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a
\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}^{+}
valued random time
n^{*}(\varepsilon,\delta,t_{1})
, such that with probability one, for any
\tau\geq 0
and
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}
,

n_{1},n_{2}<t_{1}-n^{*}\implies\|w(t_{1}+\tau;n_{1},0)-w(t_{1}+\tau;n_{2},0)\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon e^{-\delta\tau}.

We now construct the random periodic solution that has the desired attraction property.

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume
\delta_{0}>0
and let
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
. Then there exists a unique random periodic solution
w^{*}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathrm{\Omega}\rightarrow H
of system (
1.1
) such that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists positive random times
n^{*}(s,\delta)
and
n_{*}(s,\delta)
have all moments finite such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{0}\in B_{r}(w^{*}(s))}\|w(t;s,w_{0})-w^{*}(t)\|^{2}\leq r^{2}e^{-\delta(t-s)},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{\tau}\in B_{r}(w^{*}(\tau))}\|w(s;\tau,w_{\tau})-w^{*}(s)\|^{2}\leq r^{2}e^{-\delta(s-\tau)},

for all
r>0
,
t>s+n^{*}
and
\tau<s-n_{*}
.

Proof.

Let
n_{1}\in\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}
. Consider the sequence of solutions
w_{n}(t,\omega)=w(t,\omega;n_{1}-n,0)
for
n\in\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
t\geq n_{1}
. Proposition
5.2
states that there exists a random time
n^{*}(\varepsilon,\delta,n_{1})
such that for every
t\geq n_{1}
and every
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}
satisfying

m_{1},m_{2}>n^{*}
, one has

\|w(t;n_{1}-m_{1},0)-w(t;n_{1}-m_{2},0)\|\leq\varepsilon e^{-\delta(t-n_{1})}.

This implies that
\{w_{n}\}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C\left([n_{1},\infty),H\right)
, which is complete with the norm
\displaystyle\|w\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{t\geq n_{1}}\|w(t)\|
. Define
w^{*}(t,\omega)
to be the limit for
t\geq n_{1}
. Since
n_{1}
is arbitrary, we obtain a process
w^{*}(t,\omega)
defined for
t\in\mathbb{R}
. For any fixed
T>n_{1}
, it is well known (see
[
6
,
24
]
for example) that there exists a random variable
K(\omega,T)
such that
\limsup_{n}\|w_{n}(t)\|<K(\omega,T)
almost surely for all
t\in[n_{1},T]
. Therefore
w^{*}\in C([n_{1},\infty],H_{1})
, and
\{w_{n}\}
converges to
w^{*}
weakly in
H_{1}
. Hence Lemma B.6 from
[
46
]
shows that
w^{*}
is a strong solution of equation (
1.1
).

We now show that
w^{*}
is a random periodic solution in the sense of equation (
2.1
). By continuity of the stochastic flow
\mathrm{\Phi}(t,\omega;s,w_{0})
, we have for
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Phi}(t+\tau,\omega;t,w^{*}(t,\omega))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{\Phi}(t+\tau,\omega;t,\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}w_{n}(t,\omega;n_{1}-n,0))

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{\Phi}(t+\tau,\omega;t,w_{n}(t,\omega;n_{1}-n,0))

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}w_{n}(t+\tau,\omega;n_{1}-n,0)=w^{*}(t+\tau,\omega).

Denote
w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(t)=w_{n}(t,\theta_{\mathcal{T}}\omega)
. By the
\mathcal{T}
periodicity of the deterministic force
f(t)
, we have that

\displaystyle w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(t)=

\displaystyle\int_{n_{1}-n}^{t}\left[\nu\mathrm{\Delta}w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r)-B(\mathcal{K}w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r),w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r))+f(r)\right]dr+G\left[W(t,\theta_{\mathcal{T}}\omega)-W(t-n,\theta_{\mathcal{T}}\omega)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{n_{1}-n+\mathcal{T}}^{t+\mathcal{T}}\left[\nu\mathrm{\Delta}w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r-\mathcal{T})-B(\mathcal{K}w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r-\mathcal{T}),w_{n}^{\mathcal{T}}(r-\mathcal{T}))+f(r)\right]dr

\displaystyle+G\left[W(t+\mathcal{T},\omega)-W(n_{1}-n+\mathcal{T},\omega)\right].

Also note that

\displaystyle w_{n+\mathcal{T}}(t+\mathcal{T},\omega)=

\displaystyle\int_{n_{1}-n+\mathcal{T}}^{t+\mathcal{T}}\left[\nu\mathrm{\Delta}w_{n+\mathcal{T}}(r)+B(\mathcal{K}w_{n+\mathcal{T}}(r),w_{n+\mathcal{T}}(r))+f(r)\right]dr

\displaystyle+G\left[W(t+\mathcal{T},\omega)-W(n_{1}-n+\mathcal{T},\omega)\right].

Therefore it follows from the uniqueness of solution that

w_{n}(t,\theta_{\mathcal{T}}\omega)=w_{n+\mathcal{T}}(t+\mathcal{T},\omega),\,\,\mathbf{P}-\mathrm{a.s.}.

By letting
n\rightarrow\infty
, we have

w^{*}(t,\theta_{\mathcal{T}}\omega)=w^{*}(t+\mathcal{T},\omega),\,\,\mathbf{P}-\mathrm{a.s.}.

It remains to show that
w^{*}(t,\omega)
has the attraction property. Note that each
w_{n}
starts from
0
, hence estimate (
6.1
) implies that for
p>0
,
\mathbf{E}\|w_{n}(t)\|^{p}\leq C
for some constant
C
independent of
n
and
t
. This in turn shows that
\mathbf{E}\|w^{*}(t)\|^{p}\leq C
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
\varepsilon=\delta_{0}-\delta
. Define the random time
\tau_{1}=\frac{2c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\varepsilon\nu^{2}}\|w^{*}(s)\|^{2}
, which has all moments finite. Note that for
\tau>\tau_{1}
, we have
\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}\tau}\|w^{*}(s)\|^{2}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, which controls the first term in
\mathrm{\Gamma}(s,s+\tau)
from (
5.1
). Based on the same reasoning as in Proposition
5.2
, we find that there exists a
\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}^{+}
valued random time
n_{1}(s,\delta)
such that for all
\tau>n_{1}
, one has

\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}\tau}|M(s,s+\tau)|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

The estimate (
5.2
) then follows by taking
n^{*}=\max\{\tau_{1},n_{1}\}
. For
t>0
, inequality (
5.1
) states that

\|\mathfrak{e}_{s}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathfrak{e}_{s-t}\|^{2}e^{-t\left(\delta_{0}-\mathrm{\Gamma}(s-t,s)\right)},

where
\mathrm{\Gamma}(s-t,s)=\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}t}\left(\|w^{*}(s-t)\|^{2}+M(s-t,s)\right)
. Now
s
is fixed, so
M(s-t,s)
is not a martingale since it runs backwards in time. Nonetheless, we can still use the same reasoning as above. By replacing the Doob
\mathrm{L}^{p}
maximal inequality with the backwards maximal inequality (see Lemma A.6 in
[
46
]
), one obtains a
\mathcal{T}\mathbb{Z}^{+}
valued random time
n_{2}(s,\delta)
with all moments finite such that for all
t>n_{2}
,

\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}t}|M(s-t,s)|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

To estimate the term
\frac{1}{t}\|w^{*}(s-t)\|^{2}
, noting that Theorem 3.13 in
[
46
]
remains true in our setting, hence there exists a random time
\tau_{2}>0
, such that for all
t>\tau_{2}
, we have
\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{(2-\alpha)\nu^{2}t}\|w^{*}(s-t)\|^{2}<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
. Therefore the inequality (
5.3
) holds by taking
n_{*}=\max\{\tau_{2},n_{2}\}
.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

If
f(t,x)\equiv 0
, then we can take
\alpha=0
so that
\delta_{0}=\nu-\frac{c_{0}^{2}\mathcal{B}}{\nu^{2}}
which is the same as in
[
46
]
. And with the same proof as above, one can show that under the condition
\delta_{0}>0
, there is a stationary solution that exponentially attracts other solutions as in
[
46
]
.

6.
Appendix

This appendix contains certain results used in the previous sections regarding system (
1.1
). In particular, several useful bounds on the solution of equation (
1.1
) are given in subsection
6.1
. The Lyapunov structure is given in subsection
6.2
. And in subsection
6.3
, we give the proof of the technical result Proposition
3.13
about the spectral property of the Malliavin matrix. The proof of Lemma
3.15
-
3.17
are gathered in subsection
6.4
.

6.1.
Various bounds of the solution

Several estimates about the solution
w_{s,t}(w_{0})
of the stochastic Navier-Stokes equation (
1.1
) is collected in the following Lemma
6.1
. And bounds on higher order Sobolev norms are given in the next Lemma
6.2
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For the solution
w_{s,t}(w_{0})
of the Navier-Stokes equation, we have

(1)

There exist constants
\eta_{0}>0
and
C>0
depending on
f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu
such that for every
t>s
and every
\eta\in\left(0,\eta_{0}\right],
the bound

(6.1)

\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta e^{-\nu(t-s)}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

holds.

(2)

There exist positive constants
\eta_{0}
and
C
, depending on
f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu
, such that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\sup_{t\geq\tau}\left(\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}+\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr-C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})(t-\tau)\right)\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta e^{-\nu\left(\tau-s\right)}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

for every
\tau\geq s
and for every
\eta\in\left(0,\eta_{0}\right]
.

(3)

For any
f_{1},f_{2}\in H(f)
, the hull of the deterministic force, there exists a constant
\eta_{0}>0
depending on
f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu
, so that for every
\eta\in\left(0,\eta_{0}\right]
, there are positive constant
C,\gamma
depending on
f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu,\eta
, such that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}-w_{s,t}^{f_{2}}\right\|^{2}\leq Ce^{\gamma(t-s)}\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\|f_{1}-f_{2}||_{\infty},

for every
\tau\geq s
, where for
i=1,2
,
w_{s,t}^{f_{i}}
is the solution to equation (
1.1
) with
f
replaced by
f_{i}
and with initial condition
(s,w_{0})
, where
\|f_{1}-f_{2}||_{\infty}=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\|f_{1}(t)-f_{2}(t)\|
.

(4)

There exist constants
\eta_{1},a,\gamma>0,
depending on
f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu,\eta_{0}
, such that

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\sum_{n=0}^{N}\left\|w_{s,s+n}\right\|^{2}-\gamma N\right)\leq\exp\left(a\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

holds for every
N>0,
every
\eta\leq\eta_{1},
and every initial condition
w_{0}\in\mathcal{H}
.

(5)

For every
\eta>0
and
t\geq\tau\geq s
, there exists a constant
C=C\left(\nu,\eta\right)>0
such that the Jacobian
J_{\tau,t}
satisfies almost surely

(6.5)

\displaystyle\left\|J_{\tau,t}\right\|\leq\exp\left(\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+C(t-\tau)\right)

(6)

For every
\eta>0
and every
p>0,
there exists
C=C\left(f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\nu,\eta,p\right)>0
such that the Hessian satisfies

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|K_{\tau,t}\right\|^{p}\leq C\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

for every
\tau\geq s
and
t\in(\tau,\tau+1)
.

Proof.

(1)

Apply Ito’s formula and use the fact that
\langle B(\mathcal{K}w,w),w\rangle=0
. We have

(6.7)

\displaystyle\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}+2\nu\int_{s}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr=2\int_{s}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle+2\int_{s}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},f(r)\right\rangle dr+\mathcal{B}_{0}(t-s)

Let
C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})=\frac{2}{\nu}\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}+\mathcal{B}_{0}
. By the inequality
\|w\|_{1}^{2}\geq\|w\|^{2}
and Young’s product inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}-e^{-\nu(t-s)}\|w_{0}\|^{2}\leq\frac{C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})}{\nu}-\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\nu(t-r)}\|w_{s,r}\|^{2}dr+\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\nu(t-r)}\left\langle w_{s,r},2GdW(r)\right\rangle.

Since there exists constant
\alpha
such that
\frac{\nu}{2}\|w\|^{2}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|2G^{*}w\|^{2}
, so Lemma A.1 from

[
47
]
implies that

\mathbf{P}\left(\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}-e^{-\nu(t-s)}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}-\frac{C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})}{\nu}>\frac{K}{\alpha}\right)\leq e^{-K},

which is equivalent to

\mathbf{P}\left(\exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}e^{-\nu(t-s)}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})}{2\nu}\right)>e^{\frac{K}{2}}\right)\leq e^{-K}.

Now if a random variable
X
satisfies
\mathbf{P}\left(X\geq C\right)\leq\frac{1}{C^{2}}
for every
C\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}X=\int_{\mathrm{\Omega}}Xd\mathbf{P}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{0\leq X\leq 1\}}Xd\mathbf{P}+\int_{\{X\geq 1\}}Xd\mathbf{P}\leq 1+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{\{2^{n}\leq X\leq 2^{n+1}\}}Xd\mathbf{P}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{n+1}\frac{1}{2^{2n}}=5.

So
\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}e^{-\nu(t-s)}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})}{2\nu}\right)\leq 5
.
By taking
\eta_{0}=\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
C=5\exp\left(\frac{\alpha C\left(f,\mathcal{B}_{0}\right)}{2\nu}\right)
, we finish the proof.

(2)

Again apply Ito’s formula, for any
\eta>0
,
s\leq\tau<t
,

\displaystyle\eta\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\eta\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr-\eta\mathcal{B}_{0}(t-\tau)

\displaystyle=\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle-\eta\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},f(r)\right\rangle dr

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle-\frac{\eta\nu}{2}\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+\frac{2\eta}{\nu}\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(t-\tau\right)

Therefore

\displaystyle\eta\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}+\eta\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr

\displaystyle-\eta C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})(t-\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle-\frac{\eta\nu}{2}\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr

(6.9)
