\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle-\frac{\eta\alpha}{2}\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|2G^{*}w\right\|^{2}dr

Again the last inequality is obtained by choosing
\alpha>0
so that
\frac{\nu}{2}\|w\|_{1}^{2}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|2G^{*}w\|^{2}
. Setting
M(\tau,t)=\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}+2\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle w_{s,r},GdW(r)\right\rangle
, then the right hand side of inequality (
6.9
) is
M(\tau,t)-\frac{\alpha}{2\eta}[M](\tau,t)
, where
[M](\tau,t)
is the quadratic variation of the continuous
\mathrm{L}^{2}
-martingale
M
. Hence by the exponential martingale inequality, it follows that

\mathbf{P}\left(\sup_{t\geq\tau}\left(M(\tau,t)-\frac{\alpha}{2\eta}[M](\tau,t)\right)\geq K\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{\tau}\right)\leq\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha K}{\eta}\right)

for all
\tau\geq s
. As a consequence,

\mathbf{P}\left(\eta\sup_{t\geq\tau}\left(\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}+\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr-C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})(t-\tau)\right)\geq K\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{\tau}\right)\leq\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{s,\tau}\right\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha K}{\eta}\right).

In view of (
6.9
), we deduce that

\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{2}\sup_{t\geq\tau}\left(\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|^{2}+\nu\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr-C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0})(t-\tau)\right)\right)\leq 5\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\|w_{s,\tau}\|^{2}\right)

The conclusion follows from (
6.1
) by taking
\eta_{0}=\frac{\alpha}{2}
.

(3)

Let
\mathcal{R}_{t}=w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}-w_{s,t}^{f_{2}}
then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}_{t}=

\displaystyle\nu\Delta\mathcal{R}_{t}+B\left(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t}^{f_{2}},w_{s,t}^{f_{2}}\right)-B\left(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t}^{f_{1}},w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\right)+f_{2}-f_{1}

\displaystyle=\nu\Delta\mathcal{R}_{t}+B\left(\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}_{t},\mathcal{R}_{t}\right)-B\left(\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}_{t},w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\right)-B\left(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t}^{f_{1}},\mathcal{R}_{t}\right)+f_{2}-f_{1}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\nu\Delta\mathcal{R}_{t},2\mathcal{R}_{t}\right\rangle-\left\langle B\left(\mathcal{K}\mathcal{R}_{t},w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\right),2\mathcal{R}_{t}\right\rangle+\left\langle f_{2}-f_{1},2\mathcal{R}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-2\nu\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+C\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|_{1/2}\|w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\|_{1}\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|+2\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq-2\nu\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+\nu\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+C(\eta,\nu)\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|^{2}+\eta\nu\|w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\|_{1}^{2}\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|^{2}+\nu\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\nu}\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C(\eta,\nu)++\eta\nu\|w_{s,t}^{f_{1}}\|_{1}^{2}\right)\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\nu}\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}.

By the Gronwall’s inequality, the bounds (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\|\mathcal{R}_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\nu}\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\exp\Big(C(\eta,\nu)(t-\tau)\Big)\mathbf{E}\left[\exp\left(\int_{\tau}^{t}\eta\nu\|w_{s,r}^{f_{1}}\|_{1}^{2}\right)dr\right]d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\nu}\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{s}^{t}\exp\Big(C(\eta,\nu)(t-\tau)\Big)\exp\left(\eta e^{-\nu(\tau-s)}\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\gamma(t-s)}\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}

with
\gamma=C(\eta,\nu)
.

(4)

The proof of the inequality (
6.4
) is the same as that in
[
34
]
, hence we omit it here.

(5)

For any
\tau\geq s
and initial condition
\xi\in H
, the evolution of
\xi_{t}:=J_{\tau,t}\xi
is given by equation (
6.20
), which is a PDE with random coefficients. Taking
H
inner product with
\xi_{t}
and using the fact that
\langle B(\mathcal{K}w,\xi),\xi\rangle=0
, we have

\partial_{t}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}=-2\nu\left\|\nabla\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+2\langle B(\mathcal{K}\xi_{t},w_{s,t}),\xi_{t}\rangle.

Then note

\displaystyle\left|2\langle B(\mathcal{K}\xi_{t},w_{s,t}),\xi_{t}\rangle\right|\leq C\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\eta}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{\frac{1}{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\left(\varepsilon\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\right)+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}=C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\nu\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2},

by choosing
\varepsilon=\sqrt{C_{\eta}/\nu}
, where
C
depends on
\eta,\nu
.
Therefore

\partial_{t}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}\leq-\nu\left\|\nabla\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}\leq C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}.

And the result follows by the Gronwall’s inequality.

(6)

Define
\zeta_{t}=\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\nu(t-\tau)\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}^{2}
. From the equation (
6.20
) for the Jacobian
\xi_{t}
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\nu\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\langle B(\mathcal{K}\xi_{t},w_{s,t}),-\mathrm{\Delta}\xi_{t}\rangle+\langle B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t},\xi_{t}),-\mathrm{\Delta}\xi_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\nu\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{3/2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\zeta_{t}\leq

\displaystyle C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|^{2}-2(t-\tau)\nu^{2}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2(t-\tau)C\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{3/2}.

From interpolation inequalities, one has

\displaystyle 2C\|w\|_{1}\|\xi\|_{1}\|\xi\|_{3/2}\leq C_{\eta}\left\|\xi\right\|_{3/2}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\xi\right\|_{1}^{2}\leq\nu\|\xi\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\xi\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\|w\|_{1}^{2}\|\xi\|_{1}^{2}.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\zeta_{t}\leq C\left\|\zeta_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\left\|\zeta_{t}\right\|^{2}.

Hence by Gronwall’s inequality, for
s\leq\tau<t\leq s+T
,

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left\|\xi_{t}\right\|_{1}^{2}\leq\frac{C}{t-\tau}\exp\left(\eta\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr\right)\left\|\xi\right\|^{2},

where
C
depends on
\nu,\eta,T
. From basic Sobolev inequalities and interpolation inequalities, one has

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{B}(u,w)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u\|_{1/2}\|w\|_{1}+\|u\|_{1}\|w\|_{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u\|^{1/2}\|u\|_{1}^{1/2}\|w\|_{1}+\|w\|^{1/2}\|w\|_{1}^{1/2}\|u\|_{1}\right).

It then follows from the definition of
K_{\tau,t}
in (
6.3.1
) that for
t\in(\tau,\tau+1)
,

\displaystyle\left\|K_{\tau,t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\|J_{r,t}\right\|\left\|J_{\tau,r}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|J_{\tau,r}\right\|_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}dr

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\eta\int_{\tau}^{\tau+1}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+C\right)\exp\left(\frac{3}{4}\eta\int_{\tau}^{\tau+1}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr\right)\int_{\tau}^{\tau+1}\frac{\tilde{C}}{\left(r-\tau\right)^{3/4}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\exp\left(\eta\int_{\tau}^{\tau+1}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr\right),

where we used inequalities (
6.5
) and (
6.10
) in the second step. The result then follows from estimate (
6.2
).

∎

The following lemma in the velocity formulation when the deterministic force
f(t,x)
is independent of
t
, has been proved in
[
42
]
. Here we give a proof in the vorticity formulation and for the time dependent force
f(t,x)
.

Lemma 6.2
.

(
Higher order Sobolev norms of solution
) For any integer
k\geq 0
, set
\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(k,t,s)=\left(t-s\right)^{k}\|w_{s,t}\|_{k}^{2}+\nu\int_{s}^{t}\left(r-s\right)^{k}\|w_{s,r}\|_{k+1}^{2}dr.
Suppose that in the equation
(
1.1
),
f\in\mathrm{L}^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R},H_{k})
and
g_{i}\in H_{k}
for
i=1,\cdots,d
. Then for any
m\geq 1
and
T>s
, there is a constant
C=C(k,m,T-s,\nu,\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}([s,T],H_{k})},\mathcal{B}_{k})>0
, such that

\mathbf{E}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(k,t,s)^{m}\leq C\exp\left(\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right).

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction as in
[
42
]
. Let
L=-\mathrm{\Delta}
,
F_{k}(w)=(t-s)^{k}\|w\|_{k}^{2}=(t-s)^{k}\langle L^{k}w,w\rangle
. We first prove the base case when
k=0
. Applying Ito’s formula to the functional
F_{0}(w_{s,t})
, and noting
\langle B(\mathcal{K}w,w),w\rangle=0
, we have for
s\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\|w_{s,t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|w_{0}\|^{2}-2\nu\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr+2\int_{s}^{t}\langle w_{s,r},f(r)\rangle dr+\mathcal{B}_{0}(t-s)+M_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{0}\|^{2}-2\nu\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr+\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|^{2}dr+\frac{2}{\nu}\|f\|^{2}_{\mathrm{L}^{2}([s,T],H)}+\mathcal{B}_{0}(T-s)+M_{t}

where
M_{t}=2\int_{s}^{t}\langle w_{s,r},\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}dW_{i}(r)\rangle
. Note that the quadratic variation process of
M_{t}
satisfies

[M]_{t}=\int_{s}^{t}4\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle w_{s,r},g_{i}\rangle^{2}dr\leq 4\mathcal{B}_{0}\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{s,r}\|^{2}dr.

If we let
\sigma_{0}=\frac{1}{4\mathcal{B}_{0}}
,
C_{0}=\frac{2}{\nu}\|f\|^{2}_{\mathrm{L}^{2}([s,T],H)}+\mathcal{B}_{0}(T-s)
, then it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)\leq\|w_{0}\|^{2}+C_{0}+M_{t}-\frac{\sigma_{0}\nu}{2}[M]_{t}

By supermartingale inequality, one has for any
K>0

\mathbf{P}\left(\sup_{t\geq s}\left(M_{t}-\frac{\sigma_{0}\nu}{2}[M]_{t}\right)\geq K\right)\leq e^{-\sigma_{0}\nu K}.

Therefore

\mathbf{P}\left(\sup_{t\geq s}\left(\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)-C_{0}-\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\geq K\right)\leq e^{-{\sigma_{0}\nu K}}

Note for non-negative randoms variables
\xi
and
\eta
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\xi^{m}\leq 2^{m}\left(\mathbf{E}\left(\xi-\eta\right)^{m}\mathbb{I}_{\{\xi>\eta\}}+\mathbf{E}\eta^{m}\right)=2^{m}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\{\xi-\eta>\lambda^{1/m}\}d\lambda+2^{m}\mathbf{E}\eta^{m}.

Therefore

(6.11)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\sup_{t\geq s}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)^{m}\leq 2^{m}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\sigma_{0}\nu\lambda^{1/m}}d\lambda+2^{m}\mathbf{E}(C_{0}+\|w_{0}\|^{2})^{m}\leq C\exp\left(\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right).

This finishes the proof of the base case.

We now assume that
k=n\geq 1
and that for
k\leq n-1
the inequality has been proved. Applying Ito’s formula to
F_{n}(w_{s,t})
we find that

\displaystyle F_{n}(w_{s,t})=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}n(r-s)^{n-1}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n}^{2}+2(r-s)^{n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},-\nu Lw_{s,r}-B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,r},w_{s,r})+f(r)\rangle dr

\displaystyle+\frac{(t-s)^{n+1}}{n+1}\mathcal{B}_{n}+\int_{s}^{t}2(r-s)^{n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}dW_{i}(r)\rangle.

Note the quadratic variation process
[M]_{t}
of the martingale
M_{t}:=\int_{s}^{t}2(r-s)^{n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}dW_{i}(r)\rangle
satisfies

\displaystyle[M]_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{s}^{t}4(r-s)^{2n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},g_{i}\rangle^{2}dr\leq 4\mathcal{B}_{n}\left(T-s\right)^{n}\int_{s}^{t}\left(r-s\right)^{n}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n}^{2}dr.

Also note

\int_{s}^{t}2(r-s)^{n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},f(r)\rangle dr\leq\int_{s}^{t}2(r-s)^{n}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n}\|f(r)\|_{n}dr\leq\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)^{n-1}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n}^{2}+(r-s)^{n+1}\|f(r)\|_{n}^{2}dr.

Applying the inequality
\|w\|_{n}\leq\|w\|_{n+1}
, and combining these estimates, it follows that

\displaystyle F_{n}(w_{s,t})\leq

\displaystyle(n+1)\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)^{n-1}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n}^{2}dr-\frac{3\nu}{2}\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)^{n}\|w_{s,r}\|_{n+1}^{2}dr

\displaystyle-2\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)^{n}\langle L^{n}w_{s,r},B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,r},w_{s,r})\rangle dr+C_{n}+N_{n}(t),

where
C_{n}=\left(T-s\right)^{n+1}\left(\|f\|^{2}_{\mathrm{L}^{2}([s,T],H_{n})}+\frac{\mathcal{B}_{n}}{n+1}\right)
,
N_{n}(t)=M_{t}-\frac{\nu\sigma_{n}}{2}[M]_{t}
and
\sigma_{n}=\frac{1}{4\mathcal{B}_{n}(T-s)^{n}}
.

When
n=1
, the nonlinear term has the following bounds by interpolation inequality,

2\langle Lw,B(\mathcal{K}w,w)\leq C\|w\|\|w\|_{1}\|w\|_{2}\leq\frac{\nu}{2}\|w\|_{2}^{2}+C\|w\|^{2}\|w\|_{1}^{2}.

Then one has

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(1,t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)+C\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)\|w_{s,r}\|^{2}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr+C_{1}+\sup_{t\geq s}N_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)+\frac{C(T-s)}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)^{2}+C_{1}+\sup_{t\geq s}N_{1}(t).

And the result then follows from the supermartingale inequality and the same argument as in the case
n=0
.
If
n\geq 2
, we use the following inequality to bound the nonlinear term

\left|\langle L^{n}w,B(\mathcal{K}w,w)\rangle\right|\leq C_{n}\|w\|_{n+1}^{\frac{4n-1}{2n}}\|w\|_{1}^{\frac{n+1}{2n}}\|w\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\nu}{2}\|w\|_{n+1}^{2}+C\|w\|_{1}^{2(n+1)}\|w\|^{2n},

which can be proved as Lemma 2.1.20 in
[
42
]
.
It then follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(n,t,s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n+1}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(n-1,t,s)+C\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)^{n}\|w_{s,r}\|^{2n}\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2n+1}dr+C_{1}+\sup_{t\geq s}N_{n}(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n+1}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(n-1,t,s)+\frac{C}{\nu}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(1,t,s)^{n}\sup_{s\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{w_{0}}(0,t,s)^{n+1}+C_{1}+\sup_{t\geq s}N_{1}(t).

Then the desired result follows by induction hypothesis and the same reasoning as above.
∎

6.2.
The Lyapunov structure

In this subsection, we prove that the Navier-Stokes system (
1.1
) has a Lyapunov Structure that shows the contraction of the dynamics at large scales. Namely, we will prove Proposition
3.1
and an it’s corollary.

Proposition 6.3

(The Lyapunov Structure)
.

Let
V(w)=\exp(\eta\|w\|)
and
\Phi_{s,s+t}
be the stochastic flow generated by the Navier-Stokes system (
1.1
). Then there exists
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
, we have the following

1. There are
\kappa>1
and
C>0
such that
\|w\|V(w)\leq CV^{\kappa}(w)
for all
w\in H
.

2. There is a constant
C>0
such that for any fixed
0<r_{0}<1

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}V^{r}(\Phi_{s,s+t}(w))(1+\|\nabla\Phi_{s,s+t}(w))\xi\|)\leq CV^{r\alpha(t)}(w)

for every
w,\xi\in H
with
\|\xi\|=1
,
r\in[r_{0},2\kappa]
and every
t\in[0,\mathcal{T}],s\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\alpha(t)=e^{-\frac{\nu}{4}t}.

3. There is a constant
C
(possibly larger) such that

(6.13)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}V^{r}(\Phi_{s,s+t}(w))\leq CV^{r\alpha(t)}(w),

for every
t\geq 0
and
r\in[r_{0},2\kappa]
. Here
\alpha(t)=\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{k}\alpha(\beta)
is an extension to
\mathbb{R}
of the function
\alpha(t)
in (
6.12
), where
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in[0,\mathcal{T})
are unique numbers such that
t=k\mathcal{T}+\beta
.

The proof of Proposition
6.3
is based on a combination of the bounds from Lemma
6.1
and the the following lemma from
[
35
]
. Although the Wiener process here is
d
-dimensional, which is different from that in
[
35
]
, the proof is essentially the same.

Lemma 6.4
.

[
35
]

Let
U
be a real valued semimartingale

dU(t,\omega)=F(t,\omega)dt+\langle G(t,\omega),dW(t,\omega)\rangle,

where
W(t)
is a standard two sided
d
-dimensional Brownian motion,
G(t,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is the inner product in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Assume there is a process
Z
and positive constants
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}
with
b_{2}>b_{3}
such that
F(t,\omega)\leq b_{1}-b_{2}Z(t,\omega)
,
U(t,\omega)\leq Z(t,\omega)
and

\|G(t,\omega)\|^{2}\leq b_{3}Z(t,\omega)
, then

\mathbf{E}\exp\left(U(t)+\frac{b_{2}e^{-\frac{b_{2}(t-s)}{4}}}{4}\int_{s}^{t}Z(\tau)d\tau\right)\leq\frac{b_{2}e^{4b_{1}/b_{2}}}{b_{2}-b_{3}}\exp\left(U(s)e^{-\frac{b_{2}(t-s)}{4}}\right),

for all
s\in\mathbb{R},t\geq s
and deterministic initial data
U(s)
.

Proof of Proposition
6.3
.

Let
w_{s,t}=\Phi(t,\omega;s,w_{0})
be the solution of equation (
1.1
) with initial data
w_{s,s}=w_{0}
. By Ito’s formula, for
U(t)=\eta\|w_{s,t}\|^{2}
,one has

\displaystyle dU(t)

\displaystyle=\eta\left(\mathcal{B}_{0}+2\langle w_{s,t},f(t)\rangle-2\nu\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}^{2}\right)dt+\eta dM(t)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left(\mathcal{B}_{0}+\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}/\nu-\eta\nu\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}^{2}\right)dt+\eta dM(t)

where
M(t)=2\int_{s}^{t}\langle w_{s,r},\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}dW_{i}(r)\rangle
whose quadratic variation satisfies
[M](t)=4\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle w_{s,t},g_{i}\rangle^{2}\leq 4\mathcal{B}_{0}\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}^{2}
. Setting
Z(t)=\eta\|w_{s,t}\|^{2}_{1}
,
b_{1}=\eta\left(\mathcal{B}_{0}+\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}/\nu\right)
,
b_{2}=\nu
and
b_{3}=4\mathcal{B}_{0}\eta^{2}
, then
U(t)\leq Z(t)
, and
F(t)=\eta\left(\mathcal{B}_{0}+2\langle w_{s,t},f(t)\rangle-2\nu\|w_{s,t}\|_{1}^{2}\right)\leq b_{1}-b_{2}2Z(t)
, and
\|G(t)\|_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{2}=4\eta^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle w_{s,t},g_{i}\rangle^{2}\leq b_{3}Z(t)
. Therefore by Lemma
6.4
, we have for every
\eta<\sqrt{\frac{\nu}{4\mathcal{B}_{0}}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\|w_{s,t}\|^{2}+\frac{\nu}{4}e^{-\frac{\nu}{4}(t-s)}\int_{s}^{t}\eta\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}e^{-\frac{\nu(t-s)}{4}}\right),

where
C=\frac{\nu\exp\left(\frac{4\eta\left(\nu\mathcal{B}_{0}+\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)}{\nu^{2}}\right)}{\nu-4\mathcal{B}_{0}\eta^{2}}
. From the bound on the Jacobian (
6.5
), we have that for
t\in[0,\mathcal{T}]
and any
\beta>0
, there is a constant
C=C(\nu,\beta,\mathcal{T})
such that

\|\nabla w_{s,s+t}(w_{0})\|\leq C\exp\left(\beta\int_{s}^{s+t}\|w_{s,\tau}\|_{1}^{2}d\tau\right).

Therefore by taking
\beta=\frac{\nu\eta}{4}e^{-\frac{\nu\mathcal{T}}{4}}
, one has

\mathbf{E}\exp\left(\eta\|w_{s,s+t}\|^{2}\right)\left(1+\|\nabla w_{s,s+t}(w_{0})\|\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}e^{-\frac{\nu t}{4}}\right).

The inequality (
6.12
) then folllows by noting
\alpha(t)=e^{-\frac{\nu}{4}t}.

By inequality (
6.12
), one has for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}V^{r}(w)=\mathbf{E}V^{r}(\Phi_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}(w))\leq CV^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})}(w).

Then by Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}V^{r}(w)

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}V^{r})(w)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})}(w)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathcal{P}_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}V^{r}(w)\right)^{\alpha(\mathcal{T})}\leq C^{1+\alpha(\mathcal{T})}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{2}}(w).

It follows by repeating this process that

\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}V^{r}\leq C^{\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{j}}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{n}}.

Now for any
t\geq\mathcal{T}
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}V^{r}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}\mathcal{P}_{s+\beta,s+\beta+k\mathcal{T}}V^{r}\leq\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}C^{\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{j}}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{j}}(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\beta}V^{r})^{\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{k}}\leq C^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha(\mathcal{T})}}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})^{k}\alpha(\beta)}.

The proof is then complete.
∎

With the help of this proposition, one can show the following lemma on a contraction property under the metric weighted by the Lyapunov function, which will be used in the proof of the contraction of
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}^{*}
on
\mathcal{P}(H)
. In time homogeneous case
[
35
]
, the authors proved a time one step contraction under the metric weighted by the Lyapunov function to show the contraction of the Markov semigroup on
\mathcal{P}(H)
. Here we will prove and utilize the time
\mathcal{T}
step contraction in order to adapt to the periodic structure appeared in the weak irreducibility. Recall that
\Phi_{s,s+t}
is the stochastic flow generated by Navier-Stokes system (
1.1
) and
\rho_{r}
is as defined in (
2.2
).

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
r\in[r_{0},1]
, there are constants
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
C,K>0
such that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+t}(u),\Phi_{s,s+t}(v))\leq C\rho_{r}(u,v),

(6.15)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(v))\leq\sigma^{n}\rho_{r}(u,v)+K,

for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in[0,\mathcal{T}]
and
u,v\in H
.

Proof.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there is a path
\gamma
connecting
u
and
v
such that

\rho_{r}(u,v)\leq\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\gamma(t))\|\dot{\gamma}(t)\|dt\leq\rho_{r}(u,v)+\varepsilon.

Let
\tilde{\gamma}(\tau)=\Phi_{s,s+t}(\gamma(\tau))
for
t\in[0,\mathcal{T}]
. Then by (
6.12
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+t}(u),\Phi_{s,s+t}(v))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\tilde{\gamma}(\tau))\|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(\tau)\|d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\tilde{\gamma}(\tau))\left\|\nabla\Phi_{s,s+t}(\gamma(\tau))\frac{\dot{\gamma}(\tau)}{\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|}\right\|\|\dot{{\gamma}}(\tau)\|d\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}V^{r\alpha(t)}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\leq C\rho_{r}(u,v)+C\varepsilon.

The inequality (
6.14
) then follows since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary. Now let
R
be large enough so that
CV^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})}(u)\leq\sigma V^{r}(u)
for some
\sigma\in(0,1)
and for all
u
with
\|u\|\geq R
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(v))

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\rho_{r}(u,v)+C\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))V^{r\alpha(\mathcal{T})}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau+\sigma\varepsilon

(6.16)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\rho_{r}(u,v)+CV(R)\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau+\varepsilon.

Note that if we set
u_{0},v_{0}
to be the points where
\gamma
first enters and last exits the ball
B_{R}(0)
, then we have

(6.17)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau<\rho_{r}(u_{0},v_{0})+\varepsilon.

The second inequality of (
6.17
) is true since otherwise, one has

\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\geq\rho_{r}(u_{0},v_{0})+\varepsilon,

so that
\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\geq\rho_{r}(u,v)+\varepsilon
by observing that

\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}^{c}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\geq\rho_{r}(u,u_{0})+\rho_{r}(v,v_{0}),

which contradicts to the choice of the path
\gamma(\tau)
.
Note that by considering the straight line that connects
u_{0}
and
v_{0}
, we have
\rho_{r}(u_{0},v_{0})\leq 2RV^{r}(R)
. Hence

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{I}_{B_{R}(0)}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\leq 2RV^{r}(R)+\varepsilon.

By arbitrariness of
\varepsilon
, we conclude from (
6.16
) and (
6.18
) that for
K=2CRV^{r+1}(R)
.

\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+\mathcal{T}}(v))\leq\sigma\rho_{r}(u,v)+K.

For
n>1
, we apply the Markov property. Observing that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(v))|\mathcal{F}_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left[\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(u)),\Phi_{s+(n-1)\mathcal{T},s+n\mathcal{T}}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(v)))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(v))+K.

And

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(v))|\mathcal{F}_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\mathbf{E}\left[\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-2)\mathcal{T}}(u)),\Phi_{s+(n-2)\mathcal{T},s+(n-1)\mathcal{T}}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-2)\mathcal{T}}(v)))\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+(n-2)\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+(n-2)\mathcal{T}}(v))+(\sigma+1)K.

By iterating the procedure, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho_{r}(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(u),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(v))\leq\sigma^{n}\rho_{r}(u,v)+K\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\sigma^{j},

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

From the proof we know that the Lyapunov structure in Proposition
6.3
is independent of the periodic structure of system (
1.1
). One can replace
\mathcal{T}
by any fixed positive real number to yield the same estimates.

6.3.
The gradient estimate

In this subsection, we will show that the transition operator
\mathcal{P}_{s,t}
has the following gradient estimate.

Proposition 6.6
.

Assume
A_{\infty}=H
. Then there exists constant
\eta_{0}>0
so that for every
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
and
a>0

there exists constants
C=C(\eta,a)>0
and
p\in(0,1)
such that

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w)\right\|\leq C\exp(p\eta\|w\|^{2})\left(\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}|\phi|^{2}\right)(w)}+e^{-at}\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\|\nabla\phi\|^{2}\right)(w)}\right)

for every Frechet differentiable function
\phi
, every
w\in H,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\geq 0
. Here
C(\eta,a)
does not depend on initial condition
(s,w)
and
\phi
.

Proof.

The proof is a combination of inequality (
6.29
), Proposition
6.15
and Proposition
6.16
below. Note that the proof and result do not depend on the periodicity.
∎

The general scheme for the proof of the above gradient inequality is quite standard in the time homogeneous case after the groundbreaking works
[
34
,
35
,
36
,
50
]
. However, there is no known proof for the time inhomogeneous case in the literature, hence we supply a proof in this subsection, using the same arguments as that in the time homogeneous case. We first apply the integration by parts formula from the theory of Malliavin calculus, to transfer the variation on the initial condition in a solution to a variation
v
on the Wiener path. The problem of obtaining estimate (
6.19
) is then reduced to finding an appropriate
v
with bounded cost to approximately compensate the variation on the initial condition, so that the error of the two variations in the solution goes to
0
as time goes to infinity.

The invertibility of the Malliavin matrix is crucial when constructing such a desired control
v
. However, it is not easy to verify the invertibility in the infinite dimensional case, hence the inverse of its Tikhonov regularization is taken into consideration. Moreover, since the noise here is extremely degenerate, the unstable directions of the system are not directly forced. Hence one needs an infinite dimensional version of Lie bracket condition
A_{\infty}=H
as in Hörmander’s theorem to ensure the propagation of the noise to those unstable directions and obtain a spectral estimate of the Malliavin matrix to control the dynamics on the determining modes. Since there is no existing proof of the spectral property of the the Malliavin matrix in the time inhomogeneous setting, we give a proof here.

6.3.1.
The Malliavin matrix

In this subsection, we recall several facts about the Malliavin matrix and give a specific description about the construction of the control
v
. To introduce the Malliavin derivative of the solution process, we first consider its linearized equations.

As in
[
34
,
50
]
, the linearized flow
J_{\tau,r}\xi\in C\left([\tau,t];H\right)\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}\left((\tau,t];H_{1}\right)
is the solution to the equation

(6.20)

\partial_{r}J_{\tau,r}\xi=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}J_{\tau,r}\xi+\widetilde{B}(w_{s,r},J_{\tau,r}\xi),\quad r>\tau,\quad J_{\tau,\tau}\xi=\xi.

for any
r>\tau\geq s
and
\xi\in H
, where
\widetilde{B}(u,w)=-B(\mathcal{K}u,w)-B(\mathcal{K}w,u)
, where
w_{s,r}:=w(r,\omega;s,w_{0})
is the solution of (
1.1
), starting at initial time
s
.

It is also helpful to consider the time-reversed,
H
-adjoint
U^{t,(\cdot)}(r)
of
J_{r,t}(\cdot)
to analyze the Malliavin matrix. Here we use the notation
U^{t,\varphi}(r)
to emphasize that the time
t
is the initial time and
\varphi\in H
the is the initial data, and the process
U^{t,\varphi}(r)
runs backward in time for
s\leq r\leq t
. It follows that
U^{t,\varphi}(r)\in C\left([s,t];H\right)\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}\left([s,t);H_{1}\right)
is the unique solution to the backward random PDE

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{r}U^{t,\varphi}(r)=-\nu\mathrm{\Delta}U^{t,\varphi}(r)+B\left(\mathcal{K}w_{s,r},U^{t,\varphi}(r)\right)+C\left(\mathcal{K}U^{t,\varphi}(r),w_{s,r}\right),\quad s\leq r<t,\\
U^{t,\varphi}(t)=\varphi.\end{array}\right.

Here
C\left(\mathcal{K}\left(\cdot\right),w_{s,r}\right)
is the adjoint of
B\left(\mathcal{K}\left(\cdot\right),w_{s,r}\right)
determined by the relation
\langle B\left(\mathcal{K}u,w_{s,r}\right),v\rangle=\langle C\left(\mathcal{K}v,w_{s,r}\right),u\rangle
.

The second derivative
K_{\tau,t}
of
w_{s,r}
with respect to its initial condition is the solution of the following equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}K_{\tau,t}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}K_{\tau,t}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)+\widetilde{B}\left(w_{s,t},K_{\tau,t}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)\right)+\widetilde{B}\left(J_{\tau,t}\xi^{\prime},J_{\tau,t}\xi\right),\\
K_{\tau,\tau}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)=0.\end{array}\right.

By the variation of constants formula
K_{\tau,t}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)
is given by

K_{\tau,t}\left(\xi,\xi^{\prime}\right)=\int_{\tau}^{t}J_{r,t}\widetilde{B}\left(J_{\tau,r}\xi^{\prime},J_{\tau,r}\xi\right)dr.

Note that the solution
w_{s,t}(\omega,w_{0})
is a functional of the two sided Wiener process via the Ito map
\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}:C([s,t],\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow H
with
w_{s,t}(\omega,w_{0})=\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}\left(W_{[s,t]}\right)
, where
W_{[s,t]}
is the restriction of the Wiener process on
[s,t]
. And the Cameron-Martin space associated with the Wiener space
(\mathrm{\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbf{P}})
is

\mathcal{CM}=\left\{V\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\left(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d}\right):\partial_{t}V\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\left(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d}\right),\,V(0)=0\right\},

endowed with the norm
\|V\|_{\mathcal{CM}}^{2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{t}V(t)|_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}^{2}dt
, which is a Hilbert space isometric to
\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}=\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
As section 4.1 in
[
36
]
, for any
V\in\mathcal{CM}
, denote the directional derivative of the
H
valued random variable
w_{s,t}
along the direction
V
as

\mathrm{D}\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}V:=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}(W+\varepsilon V)-\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}(W)}{\varepsilon},

which exists and satisfies

(6.25)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}V=\int_{s}^{t}J_{r,t}GV^{\prime}(r)dr.

Now for any
v\in\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}
, define
V(t)=\int_{0}^{t}v(t)dt
. Then
D^{v}w_{s,t}=D^{v}\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}:=\mathrm{D}\mathrm{\Phi}_{s,t}^{w_{0}}V
is called the Malliavin derivative of the random variable
w_{s,t}
in the direction
v
. Since
D^{v}w_{s,t}
is a (random) bounded linear operator from
\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}
to
H
, by Riesz’s representation theorem, there exists a random element,
Dw_{s,t}\in\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}\otimes H
such that for every
v\in\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}
,

D^{v}w_{s,t}=\langle Dw_{s,t},v\rangle_{\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(D_{r}w_{s,t}\right)v(r)dr.

The random element
Dw_{s,t}
is the Malliavin derivative of
w_{s,t}
, which can be regarded as a stochastic process
\left(D_{r}w_{s,t}\right)_{r\in\mathbb{R}}
with values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes H
. From equation (
6.25
), we see that
D_{r}w_{s,t}=J_{r,t}G
for
r\in[s,t]
and
D_{r}w_{s,t}=0
for
r\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[s,t]
. The operator
D:\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathrm{\Omega},\mathbb{R})\otimes H\rightarrow\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{2}(\mathrm{\Omega},\mathcal{F}_{t},\mathcal{CM}^{\prime})\otimes H
is actually a closed unbounded linear operator, which is called the Malliavin derivative. Here
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{2}
is the space of
\mathrm{L}^{2}
functions adapted to the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{t}

[
36
]
.

For any
t\geq\tau\geq s
, define the random operator
A_{\tau,t}:\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}\rightarrow H
by

A_{\tau,t}v:=\langle Dw_{s,t},v\mathbb{I}_{[\tau,t]}\rangle_{\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}}=\int_{\tau}^{t}J_{r,t}Gv(r)dr,

and its adjoint
A_{\tau,t}^{*}
by the relation
\langle A_{\tau,t}v,u\rangle_{H}=\langle v,A_{\tau,t}^{*}u\rangle_{\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}}.
Then the Malliavin matrix is defined as
M_{\tau,t}:=A_{\tau,t}A_{\tau,t}^{*}
. We have for
\xi\in H
,

(6.26)

\displaystyle\langle M_{\tau,t}\xi,\xi\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\tau}^{t}\langle J_{r,t}g_{i},\xi\rangle^{2}dr=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\tau}^{t}\left\langle g_{i},U^{t,\xi}(r)\right\rangle^{2}dr,

where the second identity is due to the fact that
U^{t,(\cdot)}(\tau)
is the adjoint of
J_{\tau,t}
in
H
, which has been proved in
[
36
,
50
]
.

For any
v\in\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}
, denote by
v_{\tau,t}=v\mathbb{I}_{[\tau,t]}
the restriction of
v
on the interval
[\tau,t]
. Set the error

(6.27)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{t}=J_{s,t}\xi-A_{s,t}v_{s,t}

caused by the infinitesimal variation on the Wiener path
W
by
v
that is used to compensate the variation on the initial condition of the solution process. Applying integration by parts formula
[
52
]
for the Malliavin derivative, we have for any Fréchet differentiable
\varphi:H\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, any initial condition
w_{0}\in H
and
\alpha:=t-s\geq 0

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,t}\varphi(w_{0}),\xi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left\langle\nabla\Big(\varphi\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\Big),\xi\right\rangle=\mathbf{E}\Big(\big(\nabla\varphi\big)\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)J_{s,t}\xi\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\Big(\big(\nabla\varphi\big)\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)A_{s,t}v_{s,t}\Big)+\mathbf{E}\Big(\big(\nabla\varphi\big)\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\mathfrak{R}_{t}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\Big(D^{v_{s,t}}\varphi\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\Big)+\mathbf{E}\Big(\big(\nabla\varphi\big)\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\mathfrak{R}_{t}\Big)

(6.28)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(\varphi\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\int_{s}^{t}v(r)dW(r)\right)+\mathbf{E}\Big(\big(\nabla\varphi\big)\big(w_{s,t}(w_{0})\big)\mathfrak{R}_{t}\Big)

(6.29)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbf{E}\left|\int_{s}^{s+\alpha}v(r)dW(r)\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\alpha}|\varphi|^{2}(w_{0})}+\sqrt{\mathcal{P}_{s,s+\alpha}\|\nabla\varphi\|^{2}(w_{0})}\left(\mathbf{E}\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+\alpha}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2},

where we used Hölder’s inequality at the last step. Fix
\|\xi\|=1
, where
\xi\in H
represents the direction of the variation of the solution on the initial condition. To show the gradient inequality, we will choose an appropriate random process
v
with sample paths in
\mathcal{CM}^{\prime}
to make sure the existence of constant
C>0
and
p\in(0,1)
, such that

(6.30)

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{E}\left|\int_{s}^{s+\tau}v(r)dW(r)\right|<C\exp(p\eta\|w_{0}\|),\\
\mathbf{E}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+\tau}\right\|\leq Ce^{-a\tau}\exp(p\eta\|w_{0}\|).\end{array}

for some
a>0
and all
\tau\geq 0,w_{0}\in H
.
Note once we fixed the initial time
s
, the values of
v
before
s
do not affect the gradient estimate (
6.29
). Hence we will set
v(r)=0
for
r<s
and mainly focus on the construction of
v
after the initial time
s
.

The proof of Proposition
6.6
is then reduced to find such an appropriate control
v
, which involves the inverse of the Malliavin matrix. However, it is unclear that if the Malliavin matrix is invertible or not in the present infinite dimensional setting, therefore we consider its Tikhonov regularization
\widetilde{M}_{\tau,t}:=M_{\tau,t}+\beta
for small constant
\beta>0
, which is invertible. For integer values
n\geq 0
, define
J_{n}=J_{s+n,s+n+1}
,
A_{n}=A_{s+n,s+n+1}
,
M_{n}=A_{n}A_{n}^{*}
,
\widetilde{M}_{n}=\beta+M_{n}
. The process
v
is then recursively defined as

\displaystyle v(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}A_{2n}^{*}\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}J_{2n}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}&\text{ for }r\in[s+2n,s+2n+1),n\geq 0,\\
0&\text{ for }r\in[s+2n+1,s+2n+2),n\geq 0,\end{array}\right.

where
\mathfrak{R}_{s}=\xi
, and
\mathfrak{R}_{t}=J_{s,t}\xi-A_{s,t}v_{s,t}
. The definition is not circular since the construction of
v(r)
for
r\in[s+2n,s+2n+2]
only requires the knowledge of
\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}
, which depends only on
v(r)
for
r\in[s,s+2n]
. For instance, known
\mathfrak{R}_{s}=\xi
, we obtain the definition of
v(r)
for
r\in[s,s+2]
from formula (
6.3.1
), and
\mathfrak{R}_{t}=J_{s,t}\xi-A_{s,t}v_{s,t}
for
t\in[s,s+2]
. Then we use
\mathfrak{R}_{s+2}
to construct
v(r)
for
r\in[s+2,s+4]
and iterate this procedure.

6.3.2.
Estimate of the error
\mathfrak{R}
and the control
v

Now it remains to check (
6.30
). This will be accomplished through Proposition
6.15
and Proposition
6.16
.
We first establish several basic lemmas.

The following lemma is a version of the well known Foias–Prodi estimate. It shows that the linearized system of equation (
1.1
) has only finite number of unstable directions along the low modes. The proof of the asymptotic regularizing inequality (
6.19
) relies on an estimate of the spectrum of the Malliavin matrix on such determining modes.

Lemma 6.7
.

For any constants
p\geq 1,T,\gamma,\eta>0,
there exists an orthogonal projection
\pi_{\ell}:=\pi_{\ell}(p,T,\gamma,\eta)
onto a finite dimensional subspace of
H
such that

(6.33)

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{E}\left\|\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)J_{s,s+T}\right\|^{p}\leq\gamma\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right),\\
\mathbf{E}\left\|J_{s,s+T}\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)\right\|^{p}\leq\gamma\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right),\end{array}

for every
w_{0}\in H
.

Proof.

Let
\{\lambda_{n}\}
be the eigenvalues of
-\mathrm{\Delta}
associated with
(
1.1
)
, and
\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}
the projection onto the subspace of
H
spanned by the first
N
eigenfunctions. Let
\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}=1-\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}
.
Since
\left\|\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}J_{s,s+T}\xi\right\|\leq\frac{1}{N}\left\|J_{s,s+T}\xi\right\|_{1}
, from bound (
6.10
) for the linearization flow and (
6.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}J_{s,s+T}\right\|^{p}\leq\frac{1}{N^{p}}\mathbf{E}\left\|J_{s,s+T}\right\|_{1}^{p}\leq\gamma\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right),

for any
\gamma>0
, by choosing
N
sufficiently large. It follows from Proposition 6.1 in
[
9
]
that

(6.34)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{B}(w_{s,r},J_{s,r}\xi)\|_{-1/4}\leq\|w_{s,r}\|\|J_{s,r}\xi\|_{1}+\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}\|J_{s,r}\xi\|.

Denote
\bar{\xi}_{N}=\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}\xi
. From inequality (
6.34
), equation (
6.20
) and using the variation of constant formula, and the analyticity of
e^{t\mathrm{\Delta}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|J_{s,s+t}\bar{\xi}_{N}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|e^{\nu t\mathrm{\Delta}}\bar{\xi}_{N}+\int_{s}^{s+t}e^{\nu(s+t-r)\Delta}\widetilde{B}(w_{s,r},J_{s,r}\bar{\xi}_{N})dr\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|e^{\nu t\mathrm{\Delta}}\bar{\xi}_{N}\|+C\int_{s}^{s+t}(s+t-r)^{-1/4}\|\widetilde{B}(w_{s,r},J_{s,r}\bar{\xi}_{N})\|_{-1/4}dr

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\nu t\lambda_{N+1}}\|\xi\|+C\sup_{r\in[s,s+T]}C(r)\int_{s}^{s+t}\left(s+t-r\right)^{-1/4}(r-s)^{-1/2}dr

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\nu t\lambda_{N+1}}\|\xi\|+C\sup_{r\in[s,s+T]}C(r)\left(\int_{s}^{s+t}\left(s+t-r\right)^{-3/4}dr\right)^{1/3}\left(\int_{s}^{s+t}(r-s)^{-3/4}dr\right)^{2/3}

(6.35)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\nu t\lambda_{N+1}}\|\xi\|+t^{1/4}C\sup_{r\in[s,s+T]}C(r),

where
C(r)=\left(\|w_{s,r}\|\|J_{s,r}\xi\|_{1}+\|J_{s,r}\xi\|\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}\right)(r-s)^{1/2}
. It then follows from the bound (
6.10
), the estimates (
6.2
) and (
6.5
) from Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
s
, such that

(6.36)

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[s,s+T]}C(r)\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right).

Hence for every
p\geq 1,\gamma>0,\eta>0
, by first choosing sufficiently small
t>0
and then choosing sufficiently large
N
, we have by (
6.35
) and (
6.5
), (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\|J_{s,s+T}\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}\|^{p}\leq\left(\mathbf{E}\|J_{s+t,s+T}\|^{2p}\mathbf{E}\|J_{s,s+t}\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}^{\perp}\|^{2p}\right)^{1/2}\leq\gamma\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right).

The proof is complete by setting
\pi_{\ell}=\mathrm{\Pi}_{N}
for such a large enough
N
.
∎

We need the following important result about the spectrum property of the Malliavin matrix over the unstable modes to have desired controls on the dynamics. The same result in the time homogeneous setting has been obtained in
[
4
,
34
,
36
,
50
]
. Since there is no known proof for a time inhomogeneous system as (
1.1
), we supply a proof in the appendix.

Theorem 6.8
.

Assume
A_{\infty}=H
. For any
p\geq 1
, positive
\alpha,\eta,n
, and any orthogonal projection
\mathrm{\Pi}:H\rightarrow H
on a finite finite dimensional subspace of
H
, there exists
C=C(p,n,\eta,\nu,\mathrm{\Pi},f,\mathcal{B})
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n,\alpha,\mathrm{\Pi},f,\mathcal{B})
such that

\mathbf{P}\left(\langle M_{s,s+n}\varphi,\varphi\rangle<\varepsilon\|\varphi\|^{2}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{p}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

holds for every
(
random
)
vector
\varphi\in H
satisfying
\left\|\mathrm{\Pi}\varphi\right\|\geq\alpha\|\varphi\|
almost surely,
for every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),s\in\mathbb{R}
, and for every
w_{0}\in H
.

Proof.

The proof is based on the approach in section 6 of
[
36
]
along with some bounds on the solution of the Navier-Stokes equation (
1.1
) and its linearization.
Fixing
T>0
, we will consider the problem in the interval
[s,s+T]
. To avoid the singularities at the terminal times, we introduce
I_{\delta}:=[s+\frac{T}{2},s+T-\delta]
, where
\delta=\frac{T}{4}\varepsilon^{r}
for
0<\varepsilon<1
and some
r>0
that will be determined later. Also for
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and for a given orthogonal projection
\mathrm{\Pi}:H\rightarrow H,
we define
S_{\alpha}\subset H
by

S_{\alpha}=\{\varphi\in H\backslash\{0\}:\|\mathrm{\Pi}\varphi\|\geq\alpha\|\varphi\|\}.

The following estimates about the process
U^{t,\varphi}
in the time homogeneous case has been proved in
[
36
]
. Since the setting is a bit different here due to time inhomogeneity, we give the proof below for the reader’s convenience.

Lemma 6.9
.

For any
\delta\in(0,T/2]
,
p>0,\eta>0
, one has the bound

\mathbf{E}\sup_{\|\varphi\|\leq 1}\left\|U^{s+T,\varphi}(s+T-\delta)-e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi\right\|^{2p}\leq C\delta^{p}\exp\left(p\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right),

\mathbf{E}\sup_{\|\varphi\|\leq 1}\left\|U^{s+T,\varphi}(s+T-\delta)-\varphi\right\|_{-1}^{2p}\leq C\delta^{p}\exp\left(p\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)

Proof.

We first reverse the time of the processes by setting
\bar{w}_{s,r}=w_{s,T+2s-r}(w_{0})
, and
\bar{U}_{r}=U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+2s-r)
. Then
\bar{U}_{r}
solves the parabolic equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{r}\bar{U}_{r}=\nu\mathrm{\Delta}\bar{U}_{r}+B\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{w}_{s,r},\bar{U}_{r}\right)-C\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{U}_{r},\bar{w}_{s,r}\right),\quad s<r\leq s+T,\\
\bar{U}_{s}=\varphi.\end{array}\right.

It then follows from the variation of constant formula that,

\bar{U}_{s+\delta}=e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi+\int_{s}^{s+\delta}e^{\nu\mathrm{\Delta}\left(s+\delta-r\right)}\left[B\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{w}_{s,r},\bar{U}_{r}\right)-C\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{U}_{r},\bar{w}_{s,r}\right)\right]dr.

Since both
\|B\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{w}_{s,r},\bar{U}_{r}\right)\|
and
\|C\left(\mathcal{K}\bar{U}_{r},\bar{w}_{s,r}\right)||
are bounded by
C\|\bar{w}_{s,r}\|_{1}\|\bar{U}_{r}\|_{1}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{U}_{s+\delta}-e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi\right\|\leq C\sup_{T+s-\delta\leq r\leq s+T}\|{w}_{s,r}\|_{1}\int_{s}^{s+\delta}\|\bar{U}_{r}\|_{1}dr.

To estimate
\|\bar{U}_{r}\|_{1}
, setting
\zeta_{r}=\|\bar{U}_{r}\|^{2}+\nu(r-s)\|\bar{U}_{r}\|_{1}^{2}
. As in the proof of inequality (
6.10
), one obtains from equation for
\bar{U}_{r}
that

(6.37)

\displaystyle\|\bar{U}_{r}\|_{1}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{r-s}}\|\varphi\|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{s+T}\eta\|w_{s,r}\|_{1}^{2}dr\right),

where
C
is a constant depending on
\nu,\eta,T
.
Now it follows from bounds for the solution (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\sup_{\|\varphi\|\leq 1}\left\|\bar{U}_{s+\delta}-e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi\right\|^{2p}\leq C\delta^{p}\exp\left(p\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right),

which is the first inequality of Lemma (
6.9
). The second inequality follows from the first one and the following fact

\left\|\bar{U}_{s+\delta}-\varphi\right\|_{-1}\leq\left\|\bar{U}_{s+\delta}-e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi\right\|_{-1}+\left\|e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi-\varphi\right\|_{-1}\leq\left\|\bar{U}_{s+\delta}-e^{\delta\nu\mathrm{\Delta}}\varphi\right\|+C\delta.

∎

The next lemma allows one to transfer the properties of
\varphi
back from the terminal time.

Lemma 6.10
.

Fix any orthogonal projection
\mathrm{\Pi}
of
H
onto a finite dimensional subspace of
H
spanned by elements of
H_{1}
. There exists a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that for every
r>0
and every
\alpha>0
, the event

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}:=\left\{\omega\in\mathrm{\Omega}:\varphi\in S_{\alpha}\Longrightarrow U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta)\in S_{c\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathrm{\Pi}U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta)\|\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\varphi\|\right\}

satisfies
\mathbf{P}\left(\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}^{c}\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{p}
for every
p\geq 1
. Note that here
C
depends on
1/r
.

Proof.

Since we have proved Lemma
6.9
, this is a reformulation of Lemma 6.15 in
[
36
]
.
∎

Since the randomness spreads over the state space through the nonlinear term, we define recursively the following sets
\{A_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
formed by the symmetrized nonlinear term
\widetilde{B}(u,w)=-B(\mathcal{K}u,w)-B(\mathcal{K}w,u)
. Set
A_{1}=\{g_{k}:1\leq k\leq d\}
, and
A_{k+1}=A_{k}\cup\{\widetilde{B}(h,g_{l}):h\in A_{k},g_{l}\in A_{1}\}
. Also define
A_{\infty}=\overline{\mathrm{span}({\cup_{k\geq 1}A_{k}})}
. Note that each
A_{k}
here, consisting of constant vector fields in
H
, is a subset of the
k
-th Hörmander bracket defined in section 6 of
[
36
]
. To each
A_{n}
we associate a quadratic form
\mathcal{Q}_{n}
by
\left\langle\varphi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}\varphi\right\rangle=\sum_{h\in\mathrm{A}_{n}}\langle\varphi,h\rangle^{2}
. Just as in
[
4
,
36
,
50
]
, it is typical to apply arguments that use local time regularity to replace the analysis of non-adapted processes. Hence for
\theta\in(0,1]
we define the following (semi-)norm for functions
g:I_{\delta}\rightarrow H
by

\|g\|_{\theta,s}:=\sup_{r,t\in I_{\delta}}\frac{\|g(t)-g(r)\|_{s}}{|t-r|^{\theta}},\quad\text{and}\quad\|g\|_{\infty,s}:=\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\|g\|_{s}.

Also for
g:I_{\delta}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we use the following notation for the corresponding norms

\|g\|_{{\infty}}=\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}|g(t)|,\quad\|g\|_{\theta}:=\sup_{r,t\in I_{\delta}}\frac{|g(t)-g(r)|}{|t-r|^{\theta}}.

The following lemma is the key to prove theorem
6.8
. The proof requires a technical result which roughly states that two distinct monomials in a Wiener polynomial cannot cancel each other out
[
4
,
36
,
50
]
.

Lemma 6.11
.

For every
p\geq 1
and integer
N>0
, there exist
0<\varepsilon_{N}<1,r_{N}>0,p_{N}>0
and
q_{N}=q_{N}(p)>0
such that, provided that
r\leq r_{N},
the event

\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,N}:=\left\{\omega\in\mathrm{\Omega}:\left\langle\varphi,{M}_{s,s+T}\varphi\right\rangle\leq\varepsilon\|\varphi\|^{2}\Longrightarrow\sup_{h\in\mathrm{A}_{N}}\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left|\left\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(t),h\right\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon^{p_{N}}\|\varphi\|\right\}

satisfies

\mathbf{P}\left(\mathrm{\Omega}^{c}_{\varepsilon,N}\right)\leq C_{q_{N}}\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{p},

for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{N}]
and
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction on
N
. It suffices to show the result for
\varphi
with unit norm
\|\varphi\|=1
. We first prove that the result is true for
A_{1}
.
Assume that
\left\langle\varphi,{M}_{s,s+T}\varphi\right\rangle\leq\varepsilon
, then by representation (
6.26
), one has

\sup_{1\leq k\leq d}\int_{I_{\delta}}\left\langle g_{k},U^{s+T,\varphi}(\tau)\right\rangle^{2}d\tau\leq\varepsilon.

Setting
R(t)=\int_{s+\frac{T}{2}}^{t}\left\langle g_{k},U^{s+T,\varphi}(\tau)\right\rangle d\tau
, Lemma 6.14 in
[
36
]
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left\langle g_{k},U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\right\rangle=\|\partial_{t}R\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 4\|R\|_{\infty}\max\left\{\frac{1}{|I_{\delta}|},\|R\|_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\partial_{t}R}\|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\max\left\{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\|{\partial_{t}R}\|_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},

where
C_{T}=4\max\left\{1,\frac{2}{\sqrt{T}},\left(\frac{T}{2}\right)^{1/4}\right\}
.
It follows from Lemma E.1 of
[
50
]
that

\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}\leq C\left(1+\|w\|_{\infty,1}^{2}+\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{\infty,1}^{2}\right).

From bounds (
6.37
), Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
, we have that for any
p^{\prime}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\|{\partial_{t}R}\|_{1}^{p^{\prime}}\leq\mathbf{E}\|g_{k}\|^{p^{\prime}}\|U^{s+T,\phi}\|_{1,0}^{p^{\prime}}\leq C\mathbf{E}\|U^{s+T,\phi}\|_{1,0}^{p^{\prime}}\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\delta^{-p^{\prime}}.

Therefore by the Markov inequality, one has for
\tilde{p}>0,\alpha>0,

\mathbf{P}\left(\|\partial_{t}R\|_{1}>\alpha\varepsilon^{-\tilde{p}}\right)=\mathbf{P}\left(\|\partial_{t}R\|_{1}^{p^{\prime}}>\alpha^{p^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{-\tilde{p}p^{\prime}}\right)\leq\alpha^{-p^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}}\mathbf{E}\|{\partial_{t}R}\|_{1}^{p^{\prime}}\leq C(\alpha,p^{\prime})\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}-rp^{\prime}}.

Then on a set
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}\subset\mathrm{\Omega}
, such that
\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{c})\leq C(\alpha,p^{\prime})\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}-rp^{\prime}}
, we have

\|\partial_{t}R\|_{1}\leq\alpha\varepsilon^{-\tilde{p}}.

Now choose
\alpha=C_{T}^{-2}
,
\tilde{p}=\frac{1}{4}
, and
r_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}
. Then provided
r<r_{1}
, for any
p\geq 1
, by choosing
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{\tilde{p}-r}
, one has on
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}
,

\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left\langle g_{k},U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\right\rangle\leq C_{T}\max\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}\right)\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}

and
\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{c})\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]
, where
\varepsilon_{1}=C_{T}^{-8/3}
.
Observe that the event set
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}
does not depend on the choice of
g_{k}\in A_{1}
and it is contained in
\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,1}
. So

\mathbf{P}({\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{c})\leq\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{c})\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}.

Hence the proof for the base case is complete with
p_{1}=r_{1}=1/8,q_{1}=p/r_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{1}=C_{T}^{-8/3}
.
∎

The inductive step is accomplished through the following lemma.

Lemma 6.12
.

For
N\geq 2
, fix
h\in A_{N-1}
, and suppose that
q:=p_{N-1}
has been given. Then for
p\geq 1
, provided
r<r_{N}
, the event

\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{N,\varepsilon}:=\left\{\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left|\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(t),h\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon^{q}\Longrightarrow\sup_{1\leq k\leq d}\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left|\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(t),\widetilde{B}(h,g_{k})\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon^{p_{N}}\right\}

satisfies
\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N}^{c})\leq C_{q_{N}}\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}
for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{N})
. Here
p_{N}=q/24
,
r_{N}=q/12
,
q_{N}=12p/q
and
\varepsilon_{N}=C_{T}^{-8/(7q)}
with
C_{T}=4\max\left\{4/T,1\right\}
.

Proof.

Let
R(t)=\partial_{t}\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(t),h\rangle
. Then Lemma 6.14 in
[
36
]
(with
\alpha=1/3
) implies that

\displaystyle\|R\|_{\infty}\leq 4\max\left\{\frac{1}{|I_{\delta}|}\varepsilon^{q},\varepsilon^{\frac{q}{4}}\|R\|_{1/3}^{3/4}\right\}\leq C_{T}\max\left\{\varepsilon^{q},\varepsilon^{\frac{q}{4}}\|R\|_{1/3}^{3/4}\right\}.

Next we show that
\|R\|_{1/3}
has a bounded expectation. As in
[
36
]
, we consider the process
v_{s,t}:=w_{s,t}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}g_{k}W_{k}(t)
since it has more time regularity. Note

R(t)=\langle-\mathrm{\Delta}h+\widetilde{B}(v_{s,t},h)+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\widetilde{B}(g_{k},h)W_{k}(t),U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\rangle.

And recall that we assumed elements of
\{g_{k}\}_{k=1}^{d}
are smooth, so each
h
has bounded
H_{s}
norm for any
s
. Observe that

\displaystyle\|\langle\widetilde{B}(v_{s,t},h),U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\rangle\|_{1/3}\leq C\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}\left(\|v_{s,t}\|_{\infty,1}+\|v_{s,t}\|_{1/3,1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}\left(\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,1}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|g_{k}\|_{1}\|W_{k}\|_{\infty}+\|\partial_{t}v_{s,t}\|_{\infty,1}\right)

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq C\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}\left(1+\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,3}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|W_{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right),

for constant
C
depends on
\|h\|_{2},\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\|f\|_{\infty,1}
, where we used the fact that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}v_{s,t}\|_{\infty,1}

\displaystyle=\|\mathrm{\Delta}w_{s,t}-B(\mathcal{K}w_{s,t},w_{s,t})+f(t)\|_{\infty,1}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,3}+C\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,3}^{2}+\|f\|_{\infty,1}\leq C\left(1+\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,3}^{2}\right).

For other terms in the expression of
R(t)
, one has

(6.39)

\displaystyle\|\langle-\mathrm{\Delta}h,U^{s+T,\varphi}\rangle\|_{1/3}\leq C\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0},

and

(6.40)

\displaystyle\|\langle\sum_{k=1}^{d}\widetilde{B}(g_{k},h)W_{k}(t),U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\rangle\|_{1/3}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|W_{k}\|_{1/3}\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}.

Therefore one obtains

\displaystyle\|R\|_{1/3}\leq C\|U^{s+T,\varphi}\|_{1,0}\left(1+\|w_{s,t}\|_{\infty,3}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|W_{k}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\|W_{k}\|_{1/3}^{2}\right).

From the proof of Lemma 7.12 in
[
50
]
, we know that
\mathbf{E}\|W_{k}\|_{\alpha}^{\gamma}<C(T,\gamma)
for all
\gamma\geq 1
and
\alpha\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
. This fact, together with the bounds for the solution from Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
, implies that for any
p^{\prime}\geq 1
,
\alpha>0
and
\tilde{p}>0

\mathbf{P}\left(\|R\|_{1/3}>\alpha\varepsilon^{-\tilde{p}}\right)\leq C(p^{\prime},\alpha)\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\delta^{-p^{\prime}}\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}}\leq C(p^{\prime},\alpha)\exp\left(\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}-p^{\prime}r}.

Therefore on a set
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,1}\subset\mathrm{\Omega}
, such that
\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,1}^{c})\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{\tilde{p}p^{\prime}-p^{\prime}r}
, we have

\|R\|_{1/3}\leq\alpha\varepsilon^{-\tilde{p}}.

Now choose
p_{N},r_{N}
and
\varepsilon_{N}
as stated in the Lemma and let
\tilde{p}=q/6
,
\alpha=C_{T}^{-4/3}
. Then on the set
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,1}
, we have

\displaystyle\|R\|_{\infty}\leq C_{T}\max\left\{\varepsilon^{q},\alpha^{\frac{3}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{q}{8}}\right\}\leq\varepsilon^{\frac{q}{8}}.

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{N}]
. Note that the
\alpha
and
\varepsilon_{N}
are determined when taking the maximum. Observing that for any
p\geq 1
, provided
r<r_{N}
, we can take
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{\tilde{p}-r}
, so that

\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,1}^{c})\leq C_{q_{N}}\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}.

Denote
R_{0}=\langle-\mathrm{\Delta}h+\widetilde{B}(v_{s,t},h),U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\rangle
and
R_{k}=\langle\widetilde{B}(g_{k},h),U^{s+T,\varphi}(t)\rangle
for
k=1,2,\cdots,d
, which are the coefficients of the Wiener polynomial
R(t)
.
Then the technical Theorem 7.1 from
[
36
]
implies that on a set
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,2}\subset\mathrm{\Omega}
, one has

\displaystyle\|R\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{q/8}\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{ either }\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq k\leq d}\left\|R_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{q/24},\\
\text{ or }\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq k\leq d}\|R_{k}\|_{1}\geq\varepsilon^{-q/72},\end{array}\right.

and
\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,2}^{c})\leq C\varepsilon^{p},
where
C
depends only on
p
and the events
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,2}
depends on the processes
A_{k}
only through the value of the highest degree of the Wiener polynomial, which is
1
here.
The Markov inequality and the estimates (
6.38
)-(
6.40
) implies that there is an event
\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,3}
, on which
\|R_{k}\|_{1}<\varepsilon^{-q/72}
for each
k
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,3}^{c})\leq C\mathbf{P}\left(\|R_{k}\|_{1}^{72p/q}\geq\varepsilon^{-p}\right)\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}.

Now observe that
\bigcap_{i=1}^{3}\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,i}\subset\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N}
, hence

\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N}^{c})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathbf{P}(\widetilde{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{\varepsilon,N,i}^{c})\leq C\exp\left({\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon^{p}.

This completes the proof of the induction step.
∎

Now we give a proof of Theorem
6.8
by combining the above lemmas.
Since
A_{\infty}=H
, by Lemma 8.3 in
[
36
]
, for any fixed finite dimensional projection
\mathrm{\Pi}
, there exists
N>0
(
N
depends on the projection
\mathrm{\Pi}
, so that
p_{N},r_{N},\varepsilon_{N}
depends on
\mathrm{\Pi}
) such that for each
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
\mathrm{\Lambda}_{\alpha}>0
, such that for every
n\geq N
,

\inf_{\varphi\in S_{\alpha}}\frac{\left|\left\langle\varphi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}\varphi\right\rangle\right|}{\|\mathrm{\Pi}\varphi\|^{2}}\geq\mathrm{\Lambda}_{\alpha}.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
6.10
and Lemma
6.11
that there exist constants
p_{N},r_{N},\varepsilon_{N},c>0
such that for every
\alpha>0
, on the set
\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,N}\bigcap\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}
, the condition

\varphi\in S_{\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle\varphi,{M}_{s,s+T}\varphi\right\rangle\leq\varepsilon\|\varphi\|^{2}

implies that

U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta)\in S_{c\alpha},\quad\|\mathrm{\Pi}U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta)\|\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\varphi\|,

\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{h\in\mathrm{A}_{N}}\sup_{t\in I_{\delta}}\left|\left\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(t),h\right\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon^{p_{N}}\|\varphi\|,

and
\mathbf{P}\left(\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,N}^{c}\bigcup\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}^{c}\right)\leq C\exp\left(\eta\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{p}
, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{N})
and
p\geq 1
.
Then it follows that on the set
\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,N}\bigcap\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}
, one has

\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\varphi\|\leq\|\mathrm{\Pi}U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta)\|\leq C\mathrm{\Lambda}_{c\alpha}^{-1/2}\sup_{h\in A_{N}}\langle U^{s+T,\varphi}(T+s-\delta),h\rangle\leq C\mathrm{\Lambda}_{c\alpha}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{p_{N}}\|\varphi\|.

This in turn shows that on
\mathrm{\Omega}_{\varepsilon,N}\bigcap\mathrm{\Omega}_{\delta,\mathrm{\Pi}}
,
\left\langle\varphi,{M}_{s,s+T}\varphi\right\rangle\leq\varepsilon\|\varphi\|^{2}
and
\varphi\in S_{\alpha}
implies that

\frac{\alpha}{2}<C\varepsilon^{p_{N}},

which is not true for
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}:=\min\left\{\varepsilon_{N},\left(\frac{\alpha}{2C}\right)^{1/p_{N}}\right\}
.

Hence
\mathbf{P}\left(\langle M_{s,s+T}\varphi,\varphi\rangle<\varepsilon\|\varphi\|^{2}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{p}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)
for
\varphi\in S_{\alpha}
.
∎

The following lemma gives a quantitative control of the error between the Malliavin matrix and its regularization.

Lemma 6.13
.

Fix
\xi\in H
and set

\zeta=\beta\left(\beta+M_{0}\right)^{-1}{J}_{0}\xi.

Then for any constants
p\geq 1
,
\gamma,\eta>0
and every finite dimensional orthogonal projector
\pi_{\ell},
there exists a small
\beta_{0}:=\beta_{0}(p,\gamma,\eta)>0
such that for every
\beta\in(0,\beta_{0}]
,

\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|^{p}\leq\gamma\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)\|\xi\|^{p}.

Proof.

For
\alpha>0
, define
A_{\alpha}:=\{\omega\in\Omega:\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|(\omega)>\alpha\|\zeta\|(\omega)\}
. Let
\zeta_{\alpha}(\omega)=\zeta(\omega)\mathbb{I}_{A_{\alpha}}(\omega)
and
\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}(\omega)=\zeta(\omega)-\zeta_{\alpha}(\omega)=\zeta(\omega)\mathbb{I}_{A_{\alpha}^{c}}(\omega)
, where
\mathbb{I}_{A}
is the characteristic function of the set
A
.
Since
\|\beta(\beta+M_{0})^{-1}\|\leq 1
, it follows from estimates (
6.2
) and (
6.5
) in Lemma
6.1
that

(6.41)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}\leq\alpha^{p}\mathbf{E}\|\zeta\|^{p}\leq\alpha^{p}\mathbf{E}\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{p}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)\|\xi\|^{p}

by choosing
\alpha
sufficiently small. Fix such an
\alpha
. We also have

\displaystyle\left\langle\zeta_{\alpha},M_{0}\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\zeta,M_{0}\zeta\right\rangle\leq\left\langle\zeta,\left(M_{0}+\beta\right)\zeta\right\rangle=\left\langle\beta\left(M_{0}+\beta\right)^{-1}{J}_{0}\xi,\beta{J}_{0}\xi\right\rangle\leq\beta\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{2}.

By Theorem
6.8
, we know that for every
p\geq 1
and
\alpha>0
, there exists a constant
C
and
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\mathbf{P}\left(\left\langle M_{0}\zeta_{\alpha},\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rangle<\varepsilon\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{p}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)

holds for every
w_{0}\in H
and every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
. Therefore

\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}}{\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{2}}>\frac{\beta}{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\left\langle M_{0}\zeta_{\alpha},\zeta_{\alpha}\right\rangle<\varepsilon\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{p}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right).

Choosing
\beta=\varepsilon^{2}
, and noting
\frac{\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|}{\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|}\leq 1
, we find that

(6.42)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\frac{\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2p}}{\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{2p}}\right)\leq\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2p}}{\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{2p}}>\frac{\beta^{p}}{\varepsilon^{p}}\right)+\frac{\beta^{p}}{\varepsilon^{p}}\leq C\varepsilon^{p}\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right).

Noting that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}\leq\mathbf{E}\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}\leq\sqrt{\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{2p}\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{-2p}\right)\mathbf{E}\left\|{J}_{0}\xi\right\|^{2p}}.

Then combining this (
6.5
) from Lemma
6.1
with (
6.42
), it follows that

(6.43)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}e^{\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}}\|\xi\|^{p}

by choosing
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, which in turn gives the desired
\beta_{0}
. The lemma then follows from (
6.41
) and (
6.43
) by observing that
\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|^{p}=\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}+\mathbf{E}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\bar{\zeta}_{\alpha}\right\|^{p}
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

By Markov property in its generalized form (see for example Theorem 9.18 in
[
21
]
), it follows from Lemma
6.13
that for each positive integer
n
and
\zeta=\beta\left(\beta+M_{n}\right)^{-1}{J}_{n}\xi
, one has

\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+n}\right)\leq\gamma e^{\eta\left\|w_{s,s+n}\right\|^{2}}\|\xi\|^{p}.

Lemma 6.14
.

For any constants
\gamma,\eta>0
and
p\geq 1,
there exists a constant
\beta_{0}:=\beta_{0}(p,\gamma,\eta)>0
such that whenever
0<\beta\leq\beta_{0}
, we have

\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2(n+1)}\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\leq\gamma e^{\eta\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p},\,\,\mathbf{P}\text{-}\mathrm{a.s}..

Proof.

The proof is mainly based on Lemma
6.13
and Lemma
6.7
. Let
\zeta=\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}{J}_{2n}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}
. Observe that

(6.44)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{s+2(n+1)}={J}_{2n+1}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+1}={J}_{2n+1}\zeta.

Also note that
\|\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}\|\leq 1
and
\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}
is
\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}
measurable. Hence by the bound (
6.5
) in Lemma
6.1
and the Markov property, one has

(6.45)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\zeta\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\leq\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p}\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|{J}_{2n}\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\leq Ce^{\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p}.

Applying Lemma
6.7
, the Hölder’s inequality and estimate (
6.45
), it follows that there exists a projection
\pi_{l}
on a finite dimensional subspace of
H
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)\zeta\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\left(1-\pi_{\ell}\right)\right\|^{2p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\zeta\right\|^{2p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)}

(6.46)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\gamma}e^{\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left(Ce^{\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p}\right)\leq\gamma e^{\eta\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p}.

From Lemma
6.13
and the Markov property, it follows that for an arbitrarily small
\tilde{\gamma}
, one can choose
\beta
sufficiently small such that

\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\leq\tilde{\gamma}e^{\frac{\eta}{2}\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\|^{p}.

Again applying Hölder’s inequality and the bound (
6.5
) on the Jacobian
{J}_{2n+1}
, one can deduce that for any
\gamma>0
,

(6.47)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\pi_{\ell}\zeta\right\|^{p}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)\leq\gamma e^{\eta\left\|w_{s+2n}\right\|^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{p}.

by choosing
\beta
sufficiently small. The proof is then complete by combining (
6.44
), (
6.46
) and (
6.47
).
∎

The following result gives a desired estimate on the error between the variations on the initial condition and that on the Wiener path.

Proposition 6.15
.

There exists an
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}],
and
a>0
there is constant
C=C(\eta,a)
, and
p\in(0,1)
such that

\left(\mathbf{E}\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\exp(p\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2})e^{-at},

for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

The proof is based on Lemma
6.14
, Lemma
6.1
and an iteration procedure.
Let
C_{n}=\frac{\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+2}\right\|^{10}}{\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{10}}
, where we set
C_{n}=0
if
\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}=0
. Note that
\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2N}\right\|^{10}=\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n}
since
\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s}\right\|=\left\|\xi\right\|=1
. Also one observes that
\left\|\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}\right\|\leq 1
, and
\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+2}\right\|\leq\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}{J}_{2n}\right\|\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|
in view of (
6.44
). So by the bound (
6.5
) on the Jacobian from Lemma
6.1
, it follows that for every
\eta>0
, there exists a constant
C:=C(\eta,\nu)
such that

(6.48)

\displaystyle C_{n}\leq\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}{J}_{2n}\right\|^{10}\leq\left\|{J}_{2n+1}\right\|^{10}\left\|{J}_{2n}\right\|^{10}\leq\exp\left(\eta\int_{s+2n}^{s+2n+2}\left\|w_{r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+C\right),\,\,\mathbf{P}\text{-}\mathrm{a.s}..

Now define for
\eta,R>0
,

C_{n,R}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}e^{-\eta R}&\text{ if }\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}\geq 2R,\\
e^{\eta R}C_{n}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Note that both
C_{n}
and
C_{n,R}
are
\mathcal{F}_{s+2n+2}
measurable. We denote

\mathrm{\Omega}_{R}:=\left\{\omega\in\mathrm{\Omega}:\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}\geq 2R\right\}

and
\overline{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{R}
its complement. It follows from Lemma
6.14
that for every
R>\eta^{-1}
, there is
\beta>0
making
\gamma
sufficiently small such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(C_{n,R}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\left(\mathbb{I}_{\mathrm{\Omega}_{R}}e^{-2\eta R}+\mathbb{I}_{\bar{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{R}}e^{2\eta R}C_{n}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)=\mathbb{I}_{\mathrm{\Omega}_{R}}e^{-2\eta R}+\mathbb{I}_{\bar{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{R}}e^{2\eta R}\mathbf{E}\left(C_{n}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2n}\right)

(6.49)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}_{\mathrm{\Omega}_{R}}e^{-2\eta R}+\mathbb{I}_{\bar{\mathrm{\Omega}}_{R}}\gamma e^{4\eta R}\leq\frac{1}{2},\,\,\mathbf{P}\text{-}\mathrm{a.s}..

It now follows from the definition of
C_{n}
and inequality (
6.48
) that

\displaystyle C_{n}\leq C_{n,R}\exp\left(\eta\int_{s+2n}^{s+2n+2}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+\eta\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}+C-\eta R\right),\,\,\mathbf{P}\text{-}\mathrm{a.s}..

Therefore by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n}\leq

\displaystyle\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n,R}^{2}+\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}\exp\left(2\eta\int_{s+2n}^{s+2n+2}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+2\eta\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}+2C-2\eta R\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n,R}^{2}+\exp\left(4\eta\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}+2N\left(C-\eta R\right)\right)

(6.50)

\displaystyle+\exp\left(4\eta\int_{s}^{s+2N}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+2N\left(C-\eta R\right)\right).

Now from inequality (
6.3.2
) one has

\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n,R}^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathcal{F}_{s+2(N-1)}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}\prod_{n=0}^{N-2}C_{n,R}^{2},\,\,\mathbf{P}\text{-}\mathrm{a.s}..

Taking conditional expectation repeatedly, one obtains

(6.51)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{n=0}^{N-1}C_{n,R}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2^{N}}.

Fix
\eta>0
such that
\eta\leq\min\{\frac{1}{4}\eta_{0}\nu,\frac{1}{4}\eta_{1}\}
. Then the bounds from inequality (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) implies that

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{E}\exp\left(4\eta\int_{s}^{s+2N}\left\|w_{s,r}\right\|_{1}^{2}dr+2N\left(C-\eta R\right)\right)\leq C\exp\left(4\eta\nu^{-1}\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}+2N(C-\eta R)\right),\\
\mathbf{E}\exp\left(4\eta\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|w_{s,s+2n}\right\|^{2}+2N\left(C-\eta R\right)\right)\leq\exp\left(4a\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}+N\left(\gamma+2C-2\eta R\right)\right).\end{array}

Choosing
R
sufficiently large such that these two terms satisfy the desired bounds. Then choose
\beta
sufficiently small so that the estimate (
6.51
) holds and hence by (
6.50
)-(
6.3.2
) we have

(6.54)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2N}\right\|^{10}\leq\frac{C\exp\left(\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}\right)}{2^{N}}

for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

Note that for
t\in[2n,2n+1)
, one has

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}

\displaystyle=J_{s+2n,s+t}J_{s,s+2n}\xi-A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}

\displaystyle=J_{s+2n,s+t}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}+J_{s+2n,s+t}A_{s,s+2n}v_{s,s+2n}-A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}

\displaystyle=J_{s+2n,s+t}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}+A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}-A_{s+2n,s+t}v_{s+2n,s+t}-A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}

\displaystyle=J_{s+2n,s+t}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}-A_{s+2n,s+t}v_{s+2n,s+t}.

Hence by the definition of
v
(
6.3.1
), and the fact that
\|A_{2n}^{*}\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}\|\leq\beta^{-1/2}
, we have that

(6.55)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}\|\leq\|J_{s+2n,s+t}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\|+\|A_{s+2n,s+t}v_{s+2n,s+t}\|\leq C\beta^{-1/2}\left(1+\sup_{\tau\in[s+2n,s+t]}\|J_{\tau,s+t}\|^{2}\right)\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\|

And for
t\in[2n+1,2n+2)
, we have

\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}=J_{s+2n,s+t}J_{s,s+2n}\xi-A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}=J_{s+2n+1,s+2n+2}J_{s,s+2n+1}\xi-A_{s,s+t}v_{s,s+t}=J_{s+2n+1,s+t}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+1}.

Note that
\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+1}\|=\|\beta\widetilde{M}_{2n}^{-1}J_{2n}\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\|\leq\|J_{2n}\|\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\|
. Hence

(6.56)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}\|\leq\sup_{\tau\in[s+2n+1,s+t]}\|J_{\tau,s+t}\|\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+1}\|\leq\sup_{\tau\in[s+2n,s+t]}\|J_{\tau,s+t}\|^{2}\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n+1}\|.

Combining the above inequalities (
6.55
) and (
6.56
) with bounds on the enstrophy (
6.2
) and Jacobian (
6.5
) and inequality (
6.54
), one has

\left(\mathbf{E}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+t}\right\|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\exp\left(p\eta\|w_{0}\|^{2}\right)e^{-at}

for some
p\in(0,1)
and all
t\geq 0,\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
.
∎

The following result shows that the cost of the variation
v
on the Wiener path can be bounded. Since the proof is the same as that in
[
34
]
once we obtain the estimate (
6.54
), we omit it here.

Proposition 6.16
.

There are constants
p\in(0,1),\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
, there exists a constant
C=C(f,\mathcal{B}_{0},\eta,\nu)
so that for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbf{E}\left|\int_{s}^{s+t}v(r)dW(r)\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\beta^{2}}e^{p\eta\left\|w_{0}\right\|^{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{E}\left\|\mathfrak{R}_{s+2n}\right\|^{10}\right)^{\frac{1}{5}}.

As a byproduct, we have the following asymptotic strong Feller property. Note that the constant
C
is independent of the initial time
s
compared with the aymptotic strong Feller property proposed in
[
19
]
.

Corollary 6.17
.

Under the same condition as in Proposition
6.6
, with
t_{n}=2n
and
\delta_{n}=2^{-n}
, we have some
\eta_{0}>0
, such that for
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0}]
, there is a constant
C=C(\eta)>0
such that

\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t_{n}}\varphi(w)\|\leq C\exp(\eta\|w\|)\left(\|\varphi\|_{\infty}+\delta_{n}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\infty}\right)

for all
\varphi\in C_{b}^{1}(H)
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\in\mathbf{N}
and
w\in H
.

Proof.

The inequality follows by (
6.3.1
), estimate (
6.54
) and Proposition
6.16
.
∎

6.4.
The proof of Lemma
3.15
-
3.17

In what follows we give a proof for Lemma
3.15
-
3.17
.

Proof of Lemma
3.15
.

By Lemma
6.5
, we know that there are constants
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
K>0
such that for any
w_{1},w_{2}
with
\rho(w_{1},w_{2})\geq L
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1}),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2}))\leq\alpha^{n}\rho(w_{1},w_{2})+K\leq(\alpha+K/L)\rho(w_{1},w_{2}).

Choosing
L
large such that
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha+K/L\in(0,1).
Then

\displaystyle d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}d(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1}),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq 1+\beta\mathbf{E}\rho(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1}),\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2}))\leq 1+\alpha_{0}\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2}).

Since
\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})>\delta
, by definition of the metric
d
, one has
d(w_{1},w_{2})=1+\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2})\geq 1+\beta L
. Therefore

\displaystyle 1-\alpha_{0}\leq(1-\alpha_{0})\frac{d(w_{1},w_{2})}{1+\beta L}=\frac{1+\alpha_{0}\beta L}{1+\beta L}d(w_{1},w_{2})-\alpha_{0}d(w_{1},w_{2})

As a result

\displaystyle d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq 1+\alpha_{0}\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2})=1-\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{0}d(w_{1},w_{2})\leq\frac{1+\alpha_{0}\beta L}{1+\beta L}d(w_{1},w_{2}),

where
\alpha_{1}:=\frac{1+\alpha_{0}\beta L}{1+\beta L}\in(0,1)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.16
.

By the Monge-Kantorovich daulity
[
8
,
55
]
, one has

d(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=\sup_{\mathrm{Lip}_{d}(\phi)\leq 1}\left|\int_{H}\phi(w)\mu_{1}(dw)-\int_{H}\phi(w)\mu_{2}(dw)\right|.

Without loss of generality, in the above formula we could assume the test function
\phi\in C_{b}^{1}(H)
and
\phi(0)=0
. Then
\mathrm{Lip}_{d}(\phi)\leq 1
implies that
\|\nabla\phi(w)\|\leq(\delta^{-1}+\beta)V(w)
. Also by Proposition
6.3
we have

|\phi(w)|\leq 1+\beta\|w\|V(w)\leq 1+\beta CV^{\kappa}(w)\leq 1+\beta CV^{\kappa}(w).

Now combining Proposition
6.3
and Proposition
6.6
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta,a)V^{p}(w)\left(\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}|\phi|^{2}\right)(w)}+e^{-at}\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\|\nabla\phi\|^{2}\right)(w)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta,a)V^{p}(w)\left[\Big(1+\beta^{2}C^{2}\mathbf{E}V^{2\kappa}\big(\Phi_{s,s+t}(w)\big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+e^{-at}(\delta^{-1}+\beta)\Big(\mathbf{E}V^{2}\big(\Phi_{s,s+t}(w)\big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta,a)V^{\kappa\alpha(t)+p}(w)(1+e^{-at}\delta^{-1})=\delta^{-1}V^{\kappa\alpha(t)+p}(w)(\delta C(\eta,a)+C(\eta,a)e^{-at}).

For any
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
, choose large
T_{0}>0
so that
C(\eta,a)e^{-at}<\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2}
for all
t\geq T_{0}
. From the formula for
\alpha(t)
in Proposition
6.3
, we see that there is a large time
T>T_{0}
such that for all
t\geq T
, one has
\kappa\alpha(t)+p<1
. Choosing
r=\max\{r_{0},\kappa\alpha(t)+p\}<1
and letting
\delta
be small such that
\delta C(\eta,a)<\frac{\alpha_{2}}{2}
, then we have

\left\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w)\right\|\leq\delta^{-1}V^{r}(w)\alpha_{2}.

Note that for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in H
and any
\varepsilon>0
, there is a differentiable path
\gamma:[0,1]\rightarrow H
with
\gamma(0)=w_{1}
and
\gamma(1)=w_{2}
such that

\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})\leq\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\leq\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})+\varepsilon.

Then

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w_{1})-\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w_{2})\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(\gamma(\tau)),\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\right\rangle d\tau\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\alpha_{2}\int_{0}^{1}V^{r}(\gamma(\tau))\|\dot{\gamma}(\tau)\|d\tau\leq\delta^{-1}\alpha_{2}\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})+\delta^{-1}\alpha_{2}\varepsilon\leq\alpha_{2}d(w_{1},w_{2})+\delta^{-1}\alpha_{2}\varepsilon,

where in the last step we use the fact that
\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})<\delta
implies
d(x,y)=\delta^{-1}\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})+\beta\rho(w_{1},w_{2})
. Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we have for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in H

\sup_{\mathrm{Lip}_{d}(\phi)\leq 1}\left|\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w_{1})-\mathcal{P}_{s,s+t}\phi(w_{2})\right|\leq\alpha_{2}d(w_{1},w_{2}).

Hence by the Monge-Kantorovich daulity,

d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\alpha_{2}d({w_{1}},{w_{2}}).

The proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.17
.

For
L,\delta>0
, and
r\in[r_{0},1)
, Lemma 3.11 of
[
35
]
shows that the set
S=\{(w_{1},w_{2}):\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})\geq\delta,\rho(w_{1},w_{2})<L\}
is a bounded set in
H\times H
. So there exists
R=R(L,\delta,r)>0
such that
S\subset\{(w_{1},w_{2}):\|w_{1}\|,\|w_{2}\|\leq R\}
. By weak irreducibility from Proposition
3.9
, we know there is
n_{0}=n_{0}(L,\delta,r)
such that for every
n\geq n_{0}
, there is positive constant
a>0
so that for any
(w_{1},w_{2})\in S
, there is a coupling
(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})
of
the transition probabilities
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1},\cdot)
and
\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2},\cdot)
, such that
\mathbf{P}\left(\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})<\frac{\delta}{2}\right)>a>0
.
Note that there is a constant
C>0
such that for any
w\in H

\rho(w,0)\leq\int_{0}^{1}V(\tau w)\|w\|d\tau\leq\|w\|V(\|w\|)\leq CV^{\kappa}(w).

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\rho(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\rho(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},0)+\mathbf{E}\rho(0,Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbf{E}V^{\kappa}\left(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}\right)+\mathbf{E}V^{\kappa}\left(Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(\mathbf{E}V^{\kappa}\left(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{1})\right)+\mathbf{E}V^{\kappa}\left(\Phi_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}(w_{2})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(V^{\kappa\alpha(n\mathcal{T})}(w_{1})+V^{\kappa\alpha(n\mathcal{T})}(w_{2}))\leq R_{n},

where
R_{n}=CV^{\kappa\alpha(n\mathcal{T})}(R)
. For given random variable
X
and a measurable set
A
, denote
\mathbf{E}(X;A)=\mathbf{E}X\mathbb{I}_{A}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}d(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})=\mathbf{E}\Big(1\wedge\frac{\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})}{\delta}\Big)+\beta\mathbf{E}\rho(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\Big(1\wedge\frac{\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})}{\delta};\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})<\frac{\delta}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{E}\Big(1\wedge\frac{\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})}{\delta};\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\Big)+\beta\mathbf{E}\rho(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}).

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\left(\rho_{r}(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\right)+\beta R_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}(1-a)+\beta R_{n}=1-\frac{a}{2}+\beta R_{n}.

Letting
\beta
small enough so that
\alpha_{3}:=1-\frac{a}{2}+\beta R_{n}<1
, then since
\rho_{r}(w_{1},w_{2})\geq\delta
implies
d(w_{1},w_{2})\geq 1
, we have

d\left(\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{1}}},\mathcal{P}_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}}^{*}\delta_{{w_{2}}}\right)\leq\mathbf{E}d(X_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}},Y_{s,s+n\mathcal{T}})\leq\alpha_{3}d(w_{1},w_{2}),

which completes the proof.
∎
